\min_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\big\{\mathcal{H}(u,v):=\langle\mathbf{L}u,v\rangle\big\},

(50)

where
u
and
v
represent mixed strategies of the Policeman and the Burglar, respectively.

The optimality condition of (
50
) is
0\in Fx+Tx
, which is a special case of (
GE
), where
x:=[u,v]
,
Fx:=[\mathbf{L}^{\top}v;-\mathbf{L}u]
, and
Tx:=[\partial{\delta}_{\mathcal{U}}(u),\partial{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}(v)]
with
\delta_{\mathcal{X}}
being the indicator of
\mathcal{X}
.
Clearly,
F
is skew-symmetric and thus monotone, but it is not average co-coercive.
To make
F
average co-coercive, we add a small regularizer so that
Fx=[\epsilon u+\mathbf{L}^{\top}v;\epsilon v-\mathbf{L}u]
for
\epsilon=10^{-8}
.
This modification does not significantly interfere with (
50
).

\mathrm{(b)}
Generating input data.

First, we choose
\textrm{dist}(i,j):=|i-j|
and set
\theta:=0.8
that reflects a reasonable probability
\mathbf{p}_{c}
of catching the Burglar.
Next, we generate a vector
\hat{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}_{+}
randomly from a standard normal distribution, followed by taking the absolute value to ensure nonnegativity.
We call this vector the nominal wealth.
Then, the wealth vector
w
is generated by
w:=|\hat{w}+\sigma\cdot\texttt{randn}(q)|
as a nonnegative random vector, where
\sigma^{2}:=0.05
is the variance of the noise.
Now, assume that
\mathbf{L}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\mathbf{L}_{s}
is the mean of
n
samples
\mathbf{L}_{s}
generated from the samples
w_{s}
of
w
for
s=1,\cdots,n
.
Using this procedure, we generate two sets of problems corresponding to two experiments as follows.

•

Experiment 1.
Choose
p_{1}:=100
houses (on a
10\times 10
grid) and
n=1000
samples, and generate
10
problem instances of size
p=2p_{1}=200
.

•

Experiment 2.
Choose
p_{1}:=225
houses (on a
15\times 15
grid) and
n=2000
samples, and also generate
10
problem instances of size
p=2p_{1}=450
.

\mathrm{(c)}
Our algorithms and their competitors.

We select the following five competitors.

•

The optimistic gradient method, e.g., in
(
Daskalakis et al., 2018
)
, abbreviated by
OG
.
It is a non-accelerated deterministic variant of Popov’s past-extragradient method.

•

The fast Krasnosel’kiǐ-Mann (KM) method in
Bot and Nguyen (2022)
;
Tran-Dinh (2024a)
, called
FKM
.
This is a Nesterov’s accelerated variant of the KM scheme.

•

The variance-reduced forward-reflected-backward splitting (FRBS) algorithm in
Alacaoglu et al. (2021)
, abbreviated by
VrFRBS
.

•

The variance-reduced extragradient (EG) algorithm in
Alacaoglu and Malitsky (2022)
, abbreviated by
VrEG
.
This is a non-accelerated variance-reduced EG method.

•

The variance-reduced Halpern fixed-point method in
Cai et al. (2024)
, called
VrHalpern
.

Since the stochastic competitors either use L-SVRG or L-SARAH, we implement two variants of our
VFOSA+
:
VFOSA+
​-Svrg
and
VFOSA+
​-Sarah
and compare them with the above competitors.
We run each experiment on
10
problem instances and report the mean of the relative FBS residual norm
\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|/\|G_{\lambda}x^{0}\|
against the number of epochs for
200
epochs.

\mathrm{(d)}
Parameter selection.

We test all the algorithms using the recommended parameters from their theory.
More specifically, for all the L-SVRG variants, we choose the probability for snapshot points
\tilde{x}^{k}
as
\mathbf{p}_{k}=\frac{1}{2n^{1/3}}
and the mini-batch size
b_{k}:=\lfloor\frac{n^{2/3}}{2}\rfloor
, while for all the L-SARAH variants, we choose
\mathbf{p}_{k}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}
and
b_{k}:=\lfloor\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2}\rfloor
.
We also choose
x^{0}:=\frac{2}{p}\cdot\textrm{ones}(p)
as the initial point in all methods so that it is feasible to (
50
).
The learning rate of both
OG
and
FKM
is
\eta=\frac{1}{L}
as suggested by their theory.
The learning rate of
VrFRBS
is
\eta=0.99\cdot\frac{1-\sqrt{1-\mathbf{p}_{k}}}{2L}
as recommended in
Alacaoglu et al. (2021)
.
The learning rate of
VrEG
is
\eta=0.99\cdot\frac{\sqrt{1-\alpha}}{L}
for
\alpha:=1-\mathbf{p}_{k}
as shown in
Alacaoglu and Malitsky (2022)
.
Note that the learning rate of both
VrFRBS
and
VrEG
was derived for the single sample case, while we use it here for the mini-batch case.
However, since
\mathbf{p}_{k}
is larger than the theoretical value
\frac{1}{n}
or
\frac{2}{n}
, this learning rate is larger than the one in their paper.
The learning rate of
VrHalpern
is
\eta:=\frac{1}{4L}
as in
Cai et al. (2024)
.
For our
VFOSA+
methods, since
\rho=0
, we choose
\mu:=0.95\cdot\frac{2}{3}
,
r:=2+\frac{1}{\mu}
,
\lambda:=\frac{1}{L}
,
\bar{\beta}:=\frac{\lambda(4-L\lambda)}{4}
, and
\beta:=\frac{(2-\mu)\bar{\beta}}{2+\mu}
as suggested by our theory in Theorem
12
.

\mathrm{(e)}
Numerical results.

The performance of all the algorithms are reported in Figure
9
.

Figure 9:
The performance of
2
variants of
VFOSA+
and 5 competitors for solving (
50
) using theoretical parameters.
The average of
10
problems in each experiment.

As shown in Figure
9
, the three accelerated methods consistently outperform their non-accelerated counterparts, including both deterministic and variance-reduced variants.
The three non-accelerated schemes:
OG
,
VrFRBS
, and
VrEG
, exhibit similar performance across both experiments when using their respective theoretical parameters.
Among the accelerated methods, the deterministic algorithm
FKM
still performs well and is comparable to our
VFOSA
+
​-Svrg
and
VrHalpern
.
However, our
VFOSA
+
​-Sarah
achieves the best performance, attaining the lowest relative residual norm among all methods.

Finally, we reduce both
\mathbf{p}_{k}
and
b_{k}
by half to obtain a smaller probability and mini-batch size, respectively.
We then rerun both experiments to evaluate how these parameters influence the performance of the stochastic methods.
The results are presented in Figure
10
.

Figure 10:
The performance of
2
variants of
VFOSA+
and 5 competitors for solving (
50
) using smaller
\mathbf{p}_{k}
and
b_{k}
.
The average of
10
problems in each experiment.

As shown in Figure
10
, the variance-reduced accelerated methods show improved performance compared to the previous run.
They also outperform the deterministic
FKM
method from the earlier experiment.
This is because the smaller values of
\mathbf{p}_{k}
and
b_{k}
increase the number of iterations within the same number of epochs, enhancing performance.

7
Conclusions

We have developed two fast operator splitting frameworks with variance reduction to solve a class of generalized equations in both finite-sum and expectation settings, covering certain nonmonotone problems.
Our methods exploit both the forward-backward and the backward-forward splitting schemes and support a wide range of variance-reduced estimators, covering both unbiased and biased instances.
We have established convergence rates of order
\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^{2}\big)
and
o\big(1/k^{2}\big)
in expectation for our methods.
Then, we have also proved almost sure
o\big(1/k^{2}\big)
convergence rates along with almost sure convergence of the iterates to a solution of our problem.
Our frameworks comprise popular estimators such as L-SVRG, SAGA, L-SARAH, and Hybrid-SGD, and we have derived oracle complexity results that match or closely approach the best-known in the literature.
Several interesting questions remain open.
For example, can our approach be extended to extragradient methods and their variants to weaken the co-coercivity of
F
?
Can adaptive schemes be developed to remove the need for estimating the co-coercivity constant
L
and the co-hypomonotonicity modulus
\rho
?
We plan to explore these topics in our future work.

Acknowledgements.

This work is partially supported by the National Science Foundation (NSF), grant no. NSF-RTG DMS-2134107 and the Office of Naval Research (ONR), grant No. N00014-23-1-2588 (2023-2026).
The author gratefully acknowledges Mr. Nghia Nguyen-Trung for his careful proofreading of this work.

Appendix A
Technical Lemmas and Proof of Lemma
3

This appendix recalls necessary technical results and gives the full proof of Lemma
3
.

A.1
Technical lemmas

We need the following technical results for our convergence analysis in the sequel.

Lemma 28

(
Bauschke and Combettes (2017)
, Lemma 5.31)
.

Let
\{\alpha_{k}\}
,
\{\zeta_{k}\}
,
\{\gamma_{k}\}
, and
\{\varepsilon_{k}\}
be nonnegative sequences such that
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma_{k}<+\infty
and
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\varepsilon_{k}<+\infty
.
In addition, for all
k\geq 0
, we assume that

\alpha_{k+1}\leq(1+\gamma_{k})\alpha_{k}-\zeta_{k}+\varepsilon_{k}.

(51)

Then, we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\alpha_{k}
exists and
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\zeta_{k}<+\infty
.

Lemma 29
.

Given a nonnegative sequence
\{\alpha_{k}\}
and
\omega\geq 0
such that
\lim_{k\to\infty}k^{\omega+1}\alpha^{k}
exists and
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}k^{\omega}\alpha_{k}<+\infty
.
Then, we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}k^{\omega+1}\alpha^{k}=0
.

Proof.

Since
\alpha_{k}\geq 0
, suppose by contradiction that
\lim_{k\to\infty}k^{\omega+1}\alpha^{k}=\alpha>0
.
For any
0<\epsilon<\alpha
, there exists
k_{0}
sufficiently large such that
k^{\omega+1}\alpha_{k}\geq\alpha-\epsilon>0
for all
k\geq k_{0}
.
Hence, we get
k^{\omega}\alpha_{k}\geq\frac{\alpha-\epsilon}{k}
.
However, since
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}k^{\omega}\alpha_{k}<+\infty
, the last relation leads to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}+\infty<\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}\frac{\alpha-\epsilon}{k}\leq\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}k^{\omega}\alpha_{k}<+\infty.\end{array}

This relation shows a contradiction.
Thus, we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}k^{\omega+1}\alpha^{k}=\alpha=0
.
∎

We also need the well-known Robbins-Siegmund supermartingale theorem
(
Robbins and Siegmund, 1971
)
, which we state it here as a technical lemma.

Lemma 30
.

Let
\{U_{k}\}
,
\{\gamma_{k}\}
,
\{V_{k}\}
and
\{E_{k}\}
be sequences of nonnegative integrable random variables on some arbitrary probability space and adapted to the filtration
\left\{\mathcal{F}_{k}\right\}_{k\geq 0}
with
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma_{k}<+\infty
and
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}E_{k}<+\infty
almost surely, and

\mathbb{E}\big[U_{k+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\big]\leq(1+\gamma_{k})U_{k}-V_{k}+E_{k},

(52)

almost surely for all
k\geq 0
.
Then,
\{U_{k}\}
almost surely converges to a random variable and
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}V_{k}<+\infty
almost surely.

The following lemma is Proposition 4.1 of
Davis (2022)
.
It was proven for a demiclosed mapping
G
, but we recall it here for the case
G
is continuous in a finite-dimensional space.

Lemma 31

(
Davis (2022)
, Proposition 4.1)
.

Suppose that
G:\mathbb{R}^{p}\to\mathbb{R}^{p}
is continuous and
\mathrm{zer}(G)\neq\emptyset
.
Let
\{x^{k}\}
be a sequence of random vectors such that for all
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(G)
, the sequence
\{\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|\}
almost surely converges to a
[0,\infty)
-valued random variable.
In addition, assume that
\{\|Gx^{k}\|\}
also almost surely converges to zero.
Then,
\{x^{k}\}
almost surely converges to a
\mathrm{zer}(G)
-valued random variable.

A.2
The proof of Lemma
3
— Equivalent reformulations of (
GE
)

Proof.

Let
A_{\lambda T}x:=\frac{1}{\lambda}(x-J_{\lambda T}x)
be the Moreau-Yosida approximation of
\lambda T
.
First, we show that
A_{\lambda T}
is
(\lambda-\rho)
-co-coercive, provided that
\lambda>\rho
.
The
co-coercivity
of
A_{\lambda T}
was proven in
Attouch et al. (2018)
, but we give a short proof here for completeness.

Indeed, for any
x
and
y
, we denote by
u:=J_{\lambda T}x
and
v:=J_{\lambda T}y
.
Then, we have
A_{\lambda T}x=\frac{1}{\lambda}(x-u)\in Tu
and
A_{\lambda T}y=\frac{1}{\lambda}(y-v)\in Tv
.
Since
T
is
\rho
-co-hypomonotone, we have
\langle A_{\lambda T}x-A_{\lambda T}y,u-v\rangle\geq-\rho\|A_{\lambda T}x-A_{\lambda T}y\|^{2}
.
Substituting
u=x-\lambda A_{\lambda T}x
and
v=y-\lambda A_{\lambda T}y
into this inequality, and rearranging the result, we get

\langle A_{\lambda T}x-A_{\lambda T}y,x-y\rangle\geq(\lambda-\rho)\|A_{\lambda T}x-A_{\lambda T}y\|^{2}
, which proves that
A_{\lambda T}
is
(\lambda-\rho)
-co-coercive, provided that
\lambda>\rho
.

(i) To prove (
10
), from (
6
) we have
A_{\lambda T}(x-\lambda Fx)=\frac{1}{\lambda}(x-\lambda Fx-J_{\lambda T}(x-\lambda Fx))=G_{\lambda}x-Fx
and
A_{\lambda}(y-\lambda Fy)=G_{\lambda}y-Fy
.
By the
(\lambda-\rho)
-
co-coercivity
of
A_{\lambda T}
, we have

\langle G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y-(Fx-Fy),x-y-\lambda(Fx-Fy)\rangle\geq(\lambda-\rho)\|G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y-(Fx-Fy)\|^{2}.

Expanding this inequality and rearranging the result, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y,x-y\rangle&\geq&(\lambda-\rho)\|G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y\|^{2}-(\lambda-2\rho)\langle G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y,Fx-Fy\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle-\rho\|Fx-Fy\|^{2}\\
&=&(\lambda-\rho)\|G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y\|^{2}-(\lambda-2\rho)\langle G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y,Fx-Fy\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{L}{\hat{L}}\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle+\frac{\hat{L}-L}{\hat{L}}\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle-\rho\|Fx-Fy\|^{2}.\end{array}

Since
F
is
\frac{1}{L}
-co-coercive by our assumption, the last inequality leads to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y,x-y\rangle&\geq&(\lambda-\rho)\|G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y\|^{2}+\big(\frac{1}{\hat{L}}-\rho\big)\|Fx-Fy\|^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }(\lambda-2\rho)\langle G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y,Fx-Fy\rangle+\frac{\hat{L}-L}{\hat{L}}\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle\\
&=&\frac{4(1-\hat{L}\rho)(\lambda-\rho)-(\lambda-2\rho)^{2}\hat{L}}{4(1-\hat{L}\rho)}\|G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y\|^{2}+\frac{\hat{L}-L}{\hat{L}}\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{1-\hat{L}\rho}{\hat{L}}\big\|Fx-Fy-\frac{(\lambda-2\rho)\hat{L}}{2(1-\hat{L}\rho)}(G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y)\big\|^{2}\\
&\geq&\frac{\lambda(4-\hat{L}\lambda)-4\rho}{4(1-\hat{L}\rho)}\|G_{\lambda}x-G_{\lambda}y\|^{2}+\frac{\hat{L}-L}{\hat{L}}\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle,\end{array}

which exactly proves (
10
), where
\bar{\beta}:=\frac{\lambda(4-\hat{L}\lambda)-4\rho}{4(1-\hat{L}\rho)}\geq 0
and
\Lambda:=\frac{\hat{L}-L}{L\hat{L}}
, provided that
\rho\hat{L}<1
and
\rho<\lambda\leq\frac{2+2\sqrt{1-\hat{L}\rho}}{\hat{L}}
.

(ii) To prove (
11
),
we denote by
x:=J_{\lambda T}u
and
y:=J_{\lambda T}v
for given
u,v\in\mathrm{dom}(T)
, where
\lambda>\rho
.
Then, we have
A_{\lambda}u:=\frac{1}{\lambda}(u-J_{\lambda T}u)=\frac{1}{\lambda}(u-x)
.
Now, by (
8
), we have
A_{\lambda}u=S_{\lambda}u-Fx
.
Similarly, we also have
A_{\lambda}v=S_{\lambda}v-Fy
.
Using these two relations and the
(\lambda-\rho)
-co-coercivity of
A_{\lambda}
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v-(Fx-Fy),u-v\rangle&\geq&(\lambda-\rho)\|S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v-(Fx-Fy)\|^{2}.\end{array}

Utilizing again
x-\lambda Fx=u-\lambda S_{\lambda}u
from (
8
),
the last inequality leads to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v,u-v\rangle&\geq&(\lambda-\rho)\|S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v\|^{2}+(\lambda-\rho)\|Fx-Fy\|^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }(\lambda-2\rho)\langle S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v,Fx-Fy\rangle+\langle Fx-Fy,u-v-\lambda(S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v)\rangle\\
&=&(\lambda-\rho)\|S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v\|^{2}-(\lambda-2\rho)\langle S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v,Fx-Fy\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{L}{\hat{L}}\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle+\frac{\hat{L}-L}{\hat{L}}\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle-\rho\|Fx-Fy\|^{2}.\end{array}

Substituting
\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle\geq\frac{1}{L}\|Fx-Fy\|^{2}
from the
\frac{1}{L}
-co-coercivity of
F
into the last inequality, we can further derive that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v,u-v\rangle&\geq&(\lambda-\rho)\|S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v\|^{2}+\big(\frac{1}{\hat{L}}-\rho\big)\|Fx-Fy\|^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }(\lambda-2\rho)\langle S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v,Fx-Fy\rangle+\frac{\hat{L}-L}{\hat{L}}\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle\\
&=&\frac{4(1-\hat{L}\rho)(\lambda-\rho)-(\lambda-2\rho)^{2}\hat{L}}{4(1-\hat{L}\rho)}\|S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v\|^{2}+\frac{(\hat{L}-L)}{\hat{L}}\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{1-\hat{L}\rho}{\hat{L}}\|Fx-Fy-\frac{(\lambda-2\rho)\hat{L}}{2(1-\hat{L}\rho)}(S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v)\|^{2}\\
&\geq&\frac{\lambda(4-\hat{L}\lambda)-4\rho}{4(1-\hat{L}\rho)}\|S_{\lambda}u-S_{\lambda}v\|^{2}+\frac{\hat{L}-L}{\hat{L}}\langle Fx-Fy,x-y\rangle.\end{array}

This proves (
11
) with
\bar{\beta}:=\frac{\lambda(4-\hat{L}\lambda)-4\rho}{4(1-\hat{L}\rho)}\geq 0
and
\Lambda:=\frac{\hat{L}-L}{L\hat{L}}\geq 0
as in Statement (ii).

(iii) Finally, since
J_{\lambda T}x=x-\lambda A_{\lambda T}x
and
J_{\lambda T}y=y-\lambda A_{\lambda T}y
, using the
(\lambda-\rho)
-
co-coercivity
of
A_{\lambda T}
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|J_{\lambda T}x-J_{\lambda T}y\|^{2}&=&\|x-y-\lambda(A_{\lambda T}x-A_{\lambda T}y)\|^{2}\\
&=&\|x-y\|^{2}-2\lambda\langle A_{\lambda T}x-A_{\lambda T}y,x-y\rangle+\lambda^{2}\|A_{\lambda T}x-A_{\lambda T}y\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\|x-y\|^{2}-\lambda\big(\lambda-2\rho)\|A_{\lambda T}x-A_{\lambda T}y\|^{2}.\end{array}

Thus, if
\lambda\geq 2\rho
, then
\|J_{\lambda T}x-J_{\lambda T}y\|\leq\|x-y\|
, implying that
J_{\lambda T}
is nonexpansive.
∎

Appendix B
The Proof of Technical Results in Section
3

We provide the full proof of all technical lemmas in the main text of Section
3
.

B.1
The proof of Lemma
5
— The L-SVRG estimator

Proof.

At the
k
-th iteration, we have
3
independent random quantities: a mini-batch
\mathcal{S}_{k}
, a mega-batch
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{k}
to form
\bar{F}\tilde{x}^{k}
, and a Bernoulli’s random variable
i_{k}
following the rule (
13
).

Define
\widehat{F}^{k}:=F\tilde{x}^{k}+\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\mathcal{S}_{k})-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k},\mathcal{S}_{k})
.
Then, from (
L-SVRG
), we have
\widetilde{F}^{k}=\widehat{F}^{k}+\bar{F}\tilde{x}^{k}-F\tilde{x}^{k}
.
By Young’s inequality, for any
\tau>0
, we can show that

\mathbb{E}_{(\mathcal{S}_{k},\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{k})}\big[\|\widetilde{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq(1+\tau)\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|\widehat{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]+\tfrac{1+\tau}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[\|\bar{F}\tilde{x}^{k}-F\tilde{x}^{k}\|^{2}\big].

(53)

For the expectation setting (

E

), we consider
X^{k}(\xi):=\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k},\xi)-(Fx^{k}-F\tilde{x}^{k})
.
Then, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[X^{k}(\xi)\big]=0
.
Since
\mathcal{S}_{k}
is i.i.d., we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|\widehat{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]&=&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|F\tilde{x}^{k}+\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\mathcal{S}_{k})-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k},\mathcal{S}_{k})-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&=&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|\frac{1}{b_{k}}\sum_{\xi_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi_{i})-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k},\xi_{i})-(Fx^{k}-F\tilde{x}^{k})\big]\|^{2}\big]\\
&=&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|\frac{1}{b_{k}}\sum_{\xi_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}X^{k}(\xi_{i})\|^{2}\big]\\
&=&\frac{1}{b_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|X^{k}(\xi)\|^{2}\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{1}{b_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k},\xi)\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

For the finite-sum case (

F

), we denote by
X^{k}_{i}:=F_{i}x^{k}-F_{i}\tilde{x}^{k}-(Fx^{k}-F\tilde{x}^{k})
.
Then,
\mathbb{E}_{i}\big[X^{k}_{i}\big]=0
.

Similar to
Pham et al. (2020, Lemma 2)
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|\widehat{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]&=&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|F\tilde{x}^{k}+\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\mathcal{S}_{k})-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k},\mathcal{S}_{k})-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&=&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|\frac{1}{b_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[F_{i}x^{k}-F_{i}\tilde{x}^{k}-(Fx^{k}-F\tilde{x}^{k})\big]\|^{2}\big]\\
&=&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|\frac{1}{b_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}X^{k}_{i}\|^{2}\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{n-b_{k}}{(n-1)b_{k}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{k}-F_{i}\tilde{x}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\ \frac{1}{b_{k}n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{k}-F_{i}\tilde{x}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&=&\frac{1}{b_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k},\xi)\|^{2}\big],\quad\textrm{where $\mathbf{F}(x,\xi):=F_{i}x$}.\end{array}

Combining either the first or second relation above and (
53
), and then taking the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides of the result, we get

\hskip-4.30554pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\widetilde{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\frac{1+\tau}{b_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k},\xi)\|^{2}\big]\big]+\tfrac{1+\tau}{\tau}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\bar{F}\tilde{x}^{k}-F\tilde{x}^{k}\|^{2}\big].\end{array}\hskip-4.30554pt

(54)

If we define
\hat{\Delta}_{k}:=\frac{1}{b_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k},\xi)\|^{2}\big]
, then (
54
) implies (
14
).

Next, for a Bernoulli’s random variable
i_{k}
following the rule (
13
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{\xi,i_{k}}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k},\xi)\|^{2}\big]&=&\mathbf{p}_{k}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\xi)\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{\penalty\ }(1-\mathbf{p}_{k})\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k-1},\xi)\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

Now, for any
c>0
, by Young’s inequality, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k-1},\xi)\|^{2}\big]&\leq&(1+c)\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(\tilde{x}^{k-1},\xi)\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\left(1+\frac{1}{c}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\xi)\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

Combining the last two expressions, taking the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides of the result, and using the definition of
\hat{\Delta}_{k}
and
b_{k-1}\leq b_{k}
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\hat{\Delta}_{k}\big]&\leq&(1+c)(1-\mathbf{p}_{k})\hat{\Delta}_{k-1}+\frac{1}{b_{k}}\left[(1-\mathbf{p}_{k})\left(1+\frac{1}{c}\right)+\mathbf{p}_{k}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\xi)\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

Let us choose
c:=\frac{(1-\alpha)\mathbf{p}_{k}}{1-\mathbf{p}_{k}}
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
Then, we get
(1+c)(1-\mathbf{p}_{k})=1-\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k}
and
(1-\mathbf{p}_{k})\left(1+\frac{1}{c}\right)+\mathbf{p}_{k}=\frac{1-(1+\alpha)\mathbf{p}_{k}+\mathbf{p}_{k}^{2}}{(1-\alpha)\mathbf{p}_{k}}\leq\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)\mathbf{p}_{k}}
for any
0\leq\mathbf{p}_{k}\leq 1
.
Hence, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\hat{\Delta}_{k}\big]&\leq&(1-\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k})\hat{\Delta}_{k-1}+\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)b_{k}\mathbf{p}_{k}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\xi)\|^{2}\big],\end{array}

which proves (
15
).

Next, since
\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[\|\bar{F}\tilde{x}^{k}-F\tilde{x}^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n_{k}}
, (
54
) implies

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\widetilde{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq(1+\tau)\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\hat{\Delta}_{k}\big]+\tfrac{(1+\tau)\sigma^{2}}{\tau n_{k}}.\end{array}

(55)

Let us define
\Delta_{k}:=(1+\tau)\hat{\Delta}_{k}+\frac{(1+\tau)\sigma^{2}}{\tau n_{k}}
.
Then, plugging
\Delta_{k}
from (
55
) into (
15
) and using
n_{k-1}\leq n_{k}
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]&\leq&(1-\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k})\Delta_{k-1}+\frac{1+\tau}{(1-\alpha)b_{k}\mathbf{p}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\xi)\|^{2}\big]+\frac{(1+\tau)\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k}\sigma^{2}}{\tau n_{k}}.\end{array}

(56)

If we choose
\tau:=1
, then using (
55
) and (
56
), we can show that
\widetilde{F}^{k}
satisfies Definition
4
with
\Delta_{k}:=2\hat{\Delta}_{k}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{n_{k}}
,
\kappa_{k}:=\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k}
,
\Theta_{k}:=\frac{2}{(1-\alpha)b_{k}\mathbf{p}_{k}}
, and
\sigma_{k}^{2}:=\frac{2\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k}\sigma^{2}}{n_{k}}
.

Finally, if
\bar{F}\tilde{x}^{k}=F\tilde{x}^{k}
, then by setting
\tau=0
in (
14
) and combining the result and (
15
), they imply that
\widetilde{F}^{k}
satisfies Definition
4
with
\Delta_{k}=\hat{\Delta}_{k}
,
\kappa_{k}=\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k}
,
\Theta_{k}=\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)b_{k}\mathbf{p}_{k}}
, and
\sigma_{k}^{2}=0
.
∎

B.2
The proof of Lemma
6
— The SAGA estimator

Proof.

Let
X^{k}_{i}:=F_{i}x^{k}-\hat{F}_{i}^{k}
for all
i\in[n]
.
Then, we have
\mathbb{E}_{i}\big[X^{k}_{i}\big]=Fx^{k}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\hat{F}^{k}_{j}
for any
i\in[n]
.
Therefore, we can derive

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|\widetilde{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]&=&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|\frac{1}{b_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}X^{k}_{i}-\big[Fx^{k}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\hat{F}_{j}^{k}\big]\|^{2}\big]\\
&=&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|\frac{1}{b_{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big(X^{k}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{i}\big[X^{k}_{i}\big]]\big)\|^{2}\big]\\
&=&\frac{1}{b_{k}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{i}\big[\|X^{k}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{i}\big[X^{k}_{i}\big]\|^{2}\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{1}{b_{k}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{i}\big[\|X_{i}^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&=&\frac{1}{nb_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{k}-\hat{F}_{i}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Taking the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides of this inequality and using
\Delta_{k}:=\frac{1}{nb_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{k}-\hat{F}_{i}^{k}\|^{2}
, we obtain the first line of (
17
).

Now, from the definition of
\Delta_{k}
and the update rule (
16
), for any
c>0
, by Young’s inequality, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]&=&\frac{1}{nb_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|F_{i}x^{k}-\hat{F}_{i}^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq:SAGA_ref_points}}{=}&\big(1-\frac{b_{k}}{n}\big)\frac{1}{nb_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{k}-\hat{F}_{i}^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{b_{k}}{n}\cdot\frac{1}{nb_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{k}-F_{i}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{(1+c)b_{k-1}}{b_{k}}\big(1-\frac{b_{k}}{n}\big)\frac{1}{nb_{k-1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{k-1}-\hat{F}_{i}^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{(1+c)}{cnb_{k}}\big(1-\frac{b_{k}}{n}\big)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{k}-F_{i}x^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{k}-F_{i}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&=&\frac{(1+c)b_{k-1}}{b_{k}}\big(1-\frac{b_{k}}{n}\big)\Delta_{k-1}+\big[\frac{1}{n}+\big(1-\frac{b_{k}}{n}\big)\frac{(1+c)}{cb_{k}}\big]\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{k}-F_{i}x^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

For any
\alpha\in(0,1)
, if we choose
c:=\frac{(n-\alpha b_{k})b_{k}}{(n-b_{k})b_{k-1}}-1
, then
\frac{(1+c)b_{k-1}}{b_{k}}(1-\frac{b_{k}}{n})=1-\frac{\alpha b_{k}}{n}
.
In addition, we can also compute
\underline{C}_{k}:=\frac{1}{n}+\big(1-\frac{b_{k}}{n}\big)\frac{(1+c)}{cb_{k}}
as
\underline{C}_{k}=\frac{1}{n}+\frac{(n-b_{k})(n-\alpha b_{k})}{n[n(b_{k}-b_{k-1})+b_{k}b_{k-1}-\alpha b_{k}^{2}]}
.
Hence, taking
\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides, we obtain from the last inequality that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]&\leq&\big(1-\frac{\alpha b_{k}}{n}\big)\Delta_{k-1}+\frac{\underline{C}_{k}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{k}-F_{i}x^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(57)

Since
b_{k-1}\geq b_{k}\geq b_{k-1}-\frac{(1-\alpha)b_{k}b_{k-1}}{2n}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}n(b_{k}-b_{k-1})+b_{k}b_{k-1}-\alpha b_{k}^{2}\geq b_{k}b_{k-1}-\alpha b_{k}^{2}-\frac{(1-\alpha)}{2}b_{k}b_{k-1}=\frac{1+\alpha}{2}b_{k}b_{k-1}-\alpha b_{k}^{2}\geq\frac{(1-\alpha)b_{k}^{2}}{4}.\end{array}

This implies that
\underline{C}_{k}\leq\frac{1}{n}+\frac{2(n-b_{k})(n-\alpha b_{k})}{n(1-\alpha)b_{k}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{n}+\frac{2n}{(1-\alpha)b_{k}^{2}}\leq\frac{(3-\alpha)n}{(1-\alpha)b_{k}^{2}}=:\Theta_{k}
.
Using this bound, we obtain from (
57
) the second bound in (
17
).

Consequently,
\widetilde{F}^{k}
satisfies the
\textbf{VR}(\kappa_{k},\Theta_{k},\Delta_{k},\sigma_{k})
property in Definition
4
with
\kappa_{k}:=\frac{\alpha b_{k}}{n}\in(0,1]
,
\Delta_{k}
and
\Theta_{k}
given above, and
\sigma_{k}^{2}=0
.
∎

B.3
The proof of Lemma
7
— The L-SARAH estimator

Proof.

We prove for the expectation setting (

E

).
The proof of the finite-sum setting (

F

) is similar to the ones in
Driggs et al. (2020)
;
Li et al. (2020)
.
Let
i_{k}
be the Bernoulli’s random variable following the switching rule in (
L-SARAH
).
Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{i_{k}}\big[\|\widetilde{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]&=&(1-\mathbf{p}_{k})\|\widetilde{F}^{k-1}+\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\mathcal{S}_{k})-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\mathcal{S}_{k})-Fx^{k}\|^{2}+\mathbf{p}_{k}\|\bar{F}x^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

By the proof of the loopless SARAH estimator
(
Li et al., 2020
, Lemma 3)
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}A_{k}&:=&\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\widetilde{F}^{k-1}+\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\mathcal{S}_{k})-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\mathcal{S}_{k})-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&=&\|\widetilde{F}^{k-1}-Fx^{k-1}\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\mathcal{S}_{k})-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\mathcal{S}_{k})\|^{2}\big]-\|Fx^{k}-Fx^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\|\widetilde{F}^{k-1}-Fx^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{b_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[\|\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\xi)\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

Taking the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides of the first estimate and combining the result with the second expression, we obtain (
18
).

Finally, note that
\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[\|\bar{F}x^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n_{k}}
and
1-\mathbf{p}_{k}\leq 1
, (
18
) shows that
\widetilde{F}^{k}
satisfies Definition
4
with
\Delta_{k}:=\|\widetilde{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}
,
\kappa_{k}=\mathbf{p}_{k}
,
\Theta_{k}:=\frac{1}{b_{k}}
, and
\sigma_{k}^{2}:=\frac{\mathbf{p}_{k}\sigma^{2}}{n_{k}}
.
However, if we choose
\bar{F}x^{k}=Fx^{k}
, then we can set
\sigma_{k}=0
since
\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[\|\bar{F}x^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]=0
.
∎

B.4
The proof of Lemma
8
— The HSGD estimator

Proof.

Let
e^{k}:=\widetilde{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}
,
X^{k}(\xi):=\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\xi)-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\xi)-(Fx^{k}-Fx^{k-1})
, and
Y^{k}:=\bar{F}x^{k}-Fx^{k}
.
Then, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\big[X^{k}(\xi)\big]=0
and
\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[Y^{k}\big]=0
by our assumption.
In addition, if we denote
X^{k}:=\frac{1}{b_{k}}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal{S}_{k}}X^{k}(\xi)
, then we also have
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|X^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\frac{1}{b_{k}}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
for
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
defined in Definition
4
.
From (
HSGD
), we can write

\begin{array}[]{lcl}e^{k}&=&\widetilde{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}=(1-\tau_{k})\widetilde{F}^{k-1}+(1-\tau_{k})[\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\mathcal{S}_{k})-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\mathcal{S}_{k})]+\tau_{k}\bar{F}x^{k}-Fx^{k}\\
&=&(1-\tau_{k})(\widetilde{F}^{k-1}-Fx^{k-1})+(1-\tau_{k})[\mathbf{F}(x^{k},\mathcal{S}_{k})-\mathbf{F}(x^{k-1},\mathcal{S}_{k})-(Fx^{k}-Fx^{k-1})]\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\tau_{k}(\bar{F}x^{k}-Fx^{k})\\
&=&(1-\tau_{k})e^{k-1}+(1-\tau_{k})X^{k}+\tau_{k}Y^{k}.\end{array}

This expression leads to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|e^{k}\|^{2}&=&(1-\tau_{k})^{2}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}+(1-\tau_{k})^{2}\|X^{k}\|^{2}+\tau_{k}^{2}\|Y^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }2(1-\tau_{k})^{2}\langle e^{k-1},X^{k}\rangle+2(1-\tau_{k})\tau_{k}\langle X^{k},Y^{k}\rangle+2(1-\tau_{k})\tau_{k}\langle e^{k-1},Y^{k}\rangle.\end{array}

Taking
\mathbb{E}_{(\mathcal{S}_{k},\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k})}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides of this expression and using
\mathbb{E}_{(\mathcal{S}_{k},\hat{S}_{k})}\big[X^{k}\big]=\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[X^{k}\mid\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}\big]\big]=0
and
\mathbb{E}_{(\mathcal{S}_{k},\hat{S}_{k})}\big[Y^{k}\big]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[Y^{k}\mid\mathcal{S}_{k}\big]\big]=0
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{(\mathcal{S}_{k},\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k})}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]&=&(1-\tau_{k})^{2}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}+(1-\tau_{k})^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|X^{k}\|^{2}\big]+\tau_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[\|Y^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{\penalty\ }2(1-\tau_{k})\tau_{k}\mathbb{E}_{(\mathcal{S}_{k},\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k})}\big[\langle X^{k},Y^{k}\rangle\big].\end{array}

(58)

Here, we have used the facts that
X^{k}
only depends on
\mathcal{S}_{k}
and
Y^{k}
only depends on
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}
.

Now, we consider two cases.

(i) If
\mathcal{S}_{k}
and
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}
are independent, then
\mathbb{E}_{(\mathcal{S}_{k},\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k})}\big[\langle X^{k},Y^{k}\rangle\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\big]=0
.
Using this fact,
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|X^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\frac{1}{b_{k}}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
, and
\delta_{k}^{2}:=\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[\|Y^{k}\|^{2}\big]
into (
58
), and then taking the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides of the result, we obtain (
19
).

(ii) If
\mathcal{S}_{k}
and
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}
are not independent, then by Young’s inequality, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}2(1-\tau_{k})\tau_{k}\mathbb{E}_{(\mathcal{S}_{k},\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k})}\big[\langle X^{k},Y^{k}\rangle\big]&\leq&(1-\tau_{k})^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|X^{k}\|^{2}\big]+\tau_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[\|Y^{k}\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

Substituting this inequality,
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{S}_{k}}\big[\|X^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\frac{1}{b_{k}}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
, and
\delta_{k}^{2}:=\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[\|Y^{k}\|^{2}\big]
into (
58
), and taking the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides of the result, we obtain (
20
).

Finally, to prove (iii), we utilize
\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{k}}\big[\delta_{k}^{2}\big]\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\hat{b}_{k}}
to obtain (
12
) in Definition
4
from (
19
) and (
20
), respectively.
∎

Appendix C
The Proof of Technical Results in Section
4

This appendix presents the full proof of technical lemmas and theorems in Section
4
.

C.1
The proof of Lemma
9

Proof.

From the first two lines of (
VFOSA+
), we can easily show that
t_{k}x^{k+1}=(t_{k}-1)x^{k}+z^{k}-t_{k}\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}
.
Rearranging this expression and using
z^{k}=z^{k+1}-\nu(x^{k+1}-y^{k})=z^{k+1}+\nu\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}
from the last line of (
VFOSA+
), we obtain

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k}(t_{k}-1)(x^{k+1}-x^{k})&=&-(t_{k}-1)(x^{k}-z^{k})-t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k},\\
t_{k}(t_{k}-1)(x^{k+1}-x^{k})&=&-t_{k}(x^{k+1}-z^{k})-t_{k}^{2}\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\\
&=&-t_{k}(x^{k+1}-z^{k+1})-t_{k}(t_{k}-\nu)\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}.\end{array}\right.

(59)

Let us define
\mathcal{E}_{k+1}:=L\langle Fx^{k+1}-Fx^{k},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle
as in Lemma
9
.
Then, from (
10
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{[1]}&:=&t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle-t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle\\
&\geq&\beta t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+(\bar{\beta}-\beta)t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\Lambda t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\mathcal{E}_{k+1}.\end{array}

Substituting (
59
) into
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{[1]}
and using
e_{\lambda}^{k}:=\widetilde{G}^{k}_{\lambda}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}
and
\theta_{k}:=\frac{t_{k}-1}{t_{k}-\nu}
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{[1]}&:=&(t_{k}-1)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle-t_{k}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k+1},x^{k+1}-z^{k+1}\rangle\\
&\geq&\eta_{k}t_{k}(t_{k}-\nu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k+1},\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\rangle-\eta_{k}t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\rangle+\beta t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }(\bar{\beta}-\beta)t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\Lambda t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\mathcal{E}_{k+1}\\
&=&\eta_{k}t_{k}(t_{k}-\nu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k+1},G_{\lambda}x^{k}\rangle-\eta_{k}t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\beta t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }(\bar{\beta}-\beta)t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\Lambda t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\mathcal{E}_{k+1}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\eta_{k}t_{k}(t_{k}-\nu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-\theta_{k}G_{\lambda}x^{k},e_{\lambda}^{k}\rangle.\end{array}

By Young’s inequality, for any
s>0
, we can derive from
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{[1]}
that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{[1]}&:=&(t_{k}-1)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle-t_{k}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k+1},x^{k+1}-z^{k+1}\rangle\\
&\geq&\eta_{k}t_{k}(t_{k}-\nu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k+1},G_{\lambda}x^{k}\rangle-\eta_{k}t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\Lambda t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\mathcal{E}_{k+1}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\beta t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+(\bar{\beta}-\beta)t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }s\beta t_{k}(t_{k}-\nu)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-\theta_{k}G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta_{k}^{2}t_{k}(t_{k}-\nu)}{4s\beta}\|e_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Substituting the following identity

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-\theta_{k}G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}&=&(1-\theta_{k})\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}\|^{2}-\theta_{k}(1-\theta_{k})\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\theta_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&=&\frac{1-\nu}{t_{k}-\nu}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}\|^{2}-\frac{(1-\nu)(t_{k}-1)}{(t_{k}-\nu)^{2}}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{t_{k}-1}{t_{k}-\nu}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\end{array}

into the last expression
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{[1]}
, one can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[1]}&:=&t_{k-1}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle-t_{k}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k+1},x^{k+1}-z^{k+1}\rangle\\
&\geq&\beta t_{k}\big[t_{k}-1-s(1-\nu)\big]\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}\|^{2}-t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\big[\eta_{k}-\beta-\frac{s\beta(1-\nu)}{t_{k}-\nu}\big]\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }t_{k}\big[\eta_{k}(t_{k}-\nu)-2\beta(t_{k}-1)\big]\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k+1},G_{\lambda}x^{k}\rangle+(t_{k-1}-t_{k}+1)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\big[\bar{\beta}-(1+s)\beta\big]t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\Lambda t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\mathcal{E}_{k+1}-\frac{\eta_{k}^{2}t_{k}(t_{k}-\nu)}{4s\beta}\|e_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Since
z^{k+1}-z^{k}=-\nu\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}
, by Young’s inequality again, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[2]}&:=&\frac{1-\mu}{2\nu\eta_{k}}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}-\frac{1-\mu}{2\nu\eta_{k+1}}\|z^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&=&\frac{1-\mu}{2\nu\eta_{k}}\big[\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}-\|z^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{(1-\mu)}{2\nu}\big(\frac{1}{\eta_{k}}-\frac{1}{\eta_{k+1}}\big)\|z^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&=&-\frac{1-\mu}{\nu\eta_{k}}\langle z^{k+1}-z^{k},z^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle-\frac{1-\mu}{2\nu\eta_{k}}\|z^{k+1}-z^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{(1-\mu)}{2\nu}\big(\frac{1}{\eta_{k}}-\frac{1}{\eta_{k+1}}\big)\|z^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&=&(1-\mu)\langle\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k},z^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle-\frac{(1-\mu)\nu\eta_{k}}{2}\|\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{(1-\mu)}{2\nu}\big(\frac{1}{\eta_{k}}-\frac{1}{\eta_{k+1}}\big)\|z^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&=&(1-\mu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},z^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle+(1-\mu)\langle e_{\lambda}^{k},z^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle-\frac{(1-\mu)\nu\eta_{k}}{2}\|\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{(1-\mu)}{2\nu}\big(\frac{1}{\eta_{k}}-\frac{1}{\eta_{k+1}}\big)\|z^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&\geq&(1-\mu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},z^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle-\frac{(1-\mu)\nu\beta(t_{k-1}-1)(t_{k}-1)}{\mu(1-\nu)}\|e_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{\mu(1-\mu)(1-\nu)}{4\nu\beta(t_{k-1}-1)(t_{k}-1)}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }(1-\mu)\nu\eta_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}-(1-\mu)\nu\eta_{k}\|e_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{(1-\mu)}{2\nu}\big(\frac{1}{\eta_{k}}-\frac{1}{\eta_{k+1}}\big)\|z^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Since
\eta_{k}=\frac{2\beta(t_{k}-1)}{t_{k}-\nu}
and
t_{k}=\mu(k+r)
due to (
28
), we have
\eta_{k}(t_{k}-\nu)-2\beta(t_{k}-1)=0
and
t_{k-1}-t_{k}+1=1-\mu
, respectively.
In addition, we also have
\frac{1}{\eta_{k}}-\frac{1}{\eta_{k+1}}=\frac{\mu(1-\nu)}{2\beta(t_{k}-1)(t_{k+1}-1)}
.
Adding
\mathcal{T}_{[1]}
to
\mathcal{T}_{[2]}
and then using the last three relations, we can derive

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[3]}&:=&t_{k-1}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle-t_{k}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k+1},x^{k+1}-z^{k+1}\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{1-\mu}{2\nu\eta_{k}}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}-\frac{1-\mu}{2\nu\eta_{k+1}}\|z^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&\geq&\beta t_{k}\big[t_{k}-1-s(1-\nu)\big]\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }\frac{\beta(t_{k}-1)}{t_{k}-\nu}\big[t_{k}(t_{k}-2+\nu-s(1-\nu))+2(1-\mu)\nu\big]\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\big[\bar{\beta}-(1+s)\beta\big]t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\Lambda t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\mathcal{E}_{k+1}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }\big[\frac{\beta t_{k}(t_{k}-1)^{2}}{s(t_{k}-\nu)}+\frac{(1-\mu)\nu\beta(t_{k-1}-1)(t_{k}-1)}{\mu(1-\nu)}+\frac{2\beta\nu(1-\mu)(t_{k}-1)}{t_{k}-\nu}\big]\|e_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }\frac{\mu(1-\mu)(1-\nu)}{4\nu\beta(t_{k-1}-1)(t_{k}-1)}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{\mu(1-\mu)(1-\nu)}{4\nu\beta(t_{k}-1)(t_{k+1}-1)}\|z^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }(1-\mu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle.\end{array}

Since
\frac{1}{\eta_{k}}+\frac{\mu(1-\nu)}{2\beta(t_{k-1}-1)(t_{k}-1)}=\frac{(t_{k}-\nu)(t_{k-1}-1)+\mu(1-\nu)}{2\beta(t_{k-1}-1)(t_{k}-1)}
, using
\mathcal{L}_{k}
from (
26
) and
\|e_{\lambda}^{k}\|\leq\|\widetilde{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}\|=\|e^{k}\|
from (
22
), the last inequality leads to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\mathcal{L}_{k+1}&\geq&\beta\varphi_{k}\cdot\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+(1-\mu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle+\Lambda t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\mathcal{E}_{k+1}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\big[\bar{\beta}-(1+s)\beta\big]t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}-\psi_{k}\|e^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}

which proves (
29
), where
\varphi_{k}
and
\psi_{k}
are respectively

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\varphi_{k}&:=&t_{k-1}\big[t_{k-1}-1-s(1-\nu)\big]-\frac{(t_{k}-1)}{t_{k}-\nu}\big[t_{k}(t_{k}-2+\nu-s(1-\nu))+2\nu(1-\mu)\big],\\
\psi_{k}&:=&\beta(t_{k}-1)\big[\frac{t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}{s(t_{k}-\nu)}+\frac{2\nu(1-\mu)}{t_{k}-\nu}+\frac{\nu(1-\mu)(t_{k-1}-1)}{\mu(1-\nu)}\big],\end{array}

as given in (
30
).
∎

C.2
The proof of Lemma
10

Proof.

First, since
F
is
\frac{1}{L}
-co-coercive, it is monotone.
From (
10
),
G_{\lambda}x^{\star}=0
for any
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
, and the monotonicity of
F
, we have
\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\geq\bar{\beta}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}
.

Next, utilizing the last inequality, (
26
), and
\eta_{k}=\frac{2\beta(t_{k}-1)}{t_{k}-\nu}
from (
28
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&:=&\beta a_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+t_{k-1}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle+\frac{(1-\mu)[(t_{k}-\nu)(t_{k-1}-1)+\mu(1-\nu)]}{4\nu\beta(t_{k-1}-1)(t_{k}-1)}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&=&\frac{\beta}{2}\big(2a_{k}-t_{k-1}^{2})\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\big[\frac{(1-\mu)[(t_{k}-\nu)(t_{k-1}-1)+\mu(1-\nu)]}{4\nu\beta(t_{k-1}-1)(t_{k}-1)}-\frac{1}{2\beta}\big]\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{\beta t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+t_{k-1}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle-t_{k-1}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},z^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle+\frac{1}{2\beta}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&=&\frac{\beta(2a_{k}-t_{k-1}^{2})}{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{(t_{k-1}-1)[(1-\mu-2\nu)t_{k}+\nu(1+\mu)]+\mu(1-\mu)(1-\nu)}{4\nu\beta(t_{k-1}-1)(t_{k}-1)}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }t_{k-1}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle+\frac{1}{2\beta}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}-\beta t_{k-1}G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\geq&\frac{\beta(2a_{k}-t_{k-1}^{2})+2\bar{\beta}t_{k-1}}{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{(t_{k-1}-1)[(1-\mu-2\nu)t_{k}+\nu(1+\mu)]+\mu(1-\mu)(1-\nu)}{4\nu\beta(t_{k-1}-1)(t_{k}-1)}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}.\end{array}\vskip-2.15277pt

Now, we have
A_{k}:=\beta(2a_{k}-t_{k-1}^{2})+2\bar{\beta}t_{k-1}=\beta t_{k-1}[t_{k-1}-2-2s(1-\nu)]+2\bar{\beta}t_{k-1}
.
Using this relation into the last estimate, we obtain (
31
).

Finally, from the definitions of
\mathcal{P}_{k}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
in (
26
), the nonnegativity of the last terms in
\mathcal{P}_{k}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
, and Assumption
1.2
, we can easily show that
\mathcal{P}_{k}\geq\mathcal{Q}_{k}\geq\mathcal{L}_{k}\geq 0
.
∎

C.3
The proof of Lemma
11

Proof.

First, taking the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides of (
29
), we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&\geq&\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\mathcal{L}_{k+1}\big]+\beta\varphi_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+(1-\mu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\big[\bar{\beta}-(1+s)\beta\big]t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\Lambda t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\mathcal{E}_{k+1}\big]-\psi_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

Here,
\psi_{k}
and
\varphi_{k}
from (
30
) are respectively given by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\psi_{k}&:=&\beta(t_{k}-1)\big[\frac{t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}{s(t_{k}-\nu)}+\frac{2\nu(1-\mu)}{t_{k}-\nu}+\frac{\nu(1-\mu)(t_{k-1}-1)}{\mu(1-\nu)}\big],\\
\varphi_{k}&:=&t_{k-1}\big[t_{k-1}-1-s(1-\nu)\big]-\frac{(t_{k}-1)}{t_{k}-\nu}\big[t_{k}(t_{k}-2+\nu-s(1-\nu))+2(1-\mu)\nu\big].\end{array}

Adding
\big[\bar{\beta}-(1+s)\beta\big]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}+\Lambda t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\mathcal{E}_{k}
to both sides of the last inequality, and using
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
from (
26
) we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{Q}_{k}&\geq&\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\mathcal{Q}_{k+1}\big]+\beta\varphi_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+(1-\mu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\big[\bar{\beta}-(1+s)\beta\big]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\Lambda t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\mathcal{E}_{k}-\psi_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

(60)

Next, from (
22
) and Definition
4
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]&=&\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|\widetilde{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\Delta_{k}\big].\end{array}

(61)

Let
\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
be defined in Definition
4
.
Then, by Assumption
1.2
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{E}_{k}&=&L\cdot\langle Fx^{k}-Fx^{k-1},x^{k}-x^{k-1}\rangle\geq\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}.\end{array}

(62)

Now, from (
12
) in Definition
4
and (
61
), for
\Gamma_{k}\geq 0
in (
26
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{(1-\kappa_{k})\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\Delta_{k-1}&\geq&\frac{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]-\frac{\Theta_{k}\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}-\frac{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\sigma_{k}^{2}\\
&\geq&\psi_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]+\frac{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)-2\psi_{k}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]-\frac{\Theta_{k}\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }\frac{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\sigma_{k}^{2}.\end{array}

Adding this inequality to (
60
), and then using (
62
) we get

\hskip-0.86108pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[1]}&:=&\mathcal{Q}_{k}+\frac{(1-\kappa_{k})\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\Delta_{k-1}\\
&\geq&\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\mathcal{Q}_{k+1}\big]+\frac{[\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)-2\psi_{k}]}{2}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]+\beta\varphi_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\big[\bar{\beta}-(1+s)\beta\big]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}+(1-\mu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\big(2\Lambda-\Theta_{k}\Gamma_{k}\big)\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}-\frac{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\sigma_{k}^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-17.22217pt

(63)

Assume that
[\Gamma_{k+1}(1-\kappa_{k+1})+\mu^{-1}\beta]t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\leq\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)-2\psi_{k}
, which is exactly the condition (
32
).
Then, using
\mathcal{P}_{k}
from (
26
) and this condition, we obtain from (
63
) that

\hskip-0.86108pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}&:=&\mathcal{Q}_{k}+\frac{[\mu(1-\kappa_{k})\Gamma_{k}+\beta]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2\mu}\Delta_{k-1}\\
&\geq&\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\mathcal{Q}_{k+1}\big]+\frac{[\mu(1-\kappa_{k+1})\Gamma_{k+1}+\beta]t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}{2\mu}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]+\frac{\beta t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2\mu}\Delta_{k-1}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\beta\varphi_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+(1-\mu)\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\big[\bar{\beta}-(1+s)\beta\big]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\big(2\Lambda-\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k}\big)\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}-\frac{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\sigma_{k}^{2}.\end{array}

Finally, using again
\mathcal{P}_{k+1}:=\mathcal{Q}_{k+1}+\frac{[\mu(1-\kappa_{k+1})\Gamma_{k+1}+\beta]t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}{2\mu}\Delta_{k}
from (
26
), the last expression implies (
33
).
∎

C.4
The proof of Theorem
12
— Key estimates

Proof.

Suppose that we choose
\nu:=\frac{\mu}{2}
,
s:=\frac{2(\mu-\nu)}{1-\nu}=\frac{2\mu}{2-\mu}
, and
0<\beta\leq\frac{\bar{\beta}}{1+s}=\frac{(2-\mu)\bar{\beta}}{2+\mu}
.
Using these choices and
t_{k}:=\mu(k+r)
from (
34
) for
r\geq 2+\frac{1}{\mu}
,
\varphi_{k}
and
\psi_{k}
defined by (
30
) respectively become

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\varphi_{k}&:=&t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1-2(\mu-\nu))-\frac{t_{k}-1}{t_{k}-\nu}\big[t_{k}^{2}-(2+2\mu-3\nu)t_{k}+2(1-\mu)\nu\big]\\
&\geq&\frac{(2-3\mu)t_{k}}{2}+3\mu^{2},\\
\psi_{k}&:=&\beta(t_{k}-1)\big[\frac{(1-\nu)t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}{2(\mu-\nu)(t_{k}-\nu)}+\frac{2(1-\mu)\nu}{t_{k}-\nu}+\frac{(1-\mu)\nu(t_{k-1}-1)}{\mu(1-\nu)}\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{2\beta}{\mu}t_{k}(t_{k}-1).\end{array}

If we choose
0<\mu<\frac{2}{3}
as in (
34
), then from the first expression, we get
\varphi_{k}>0
.

From the second expression, we can check that (
32
) of Lemma
11
holds if we impose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\big[\Gamma_{k+1}(1-\kappa_{k+1})+\frac{5\beta}{\mu}\big]t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\leq\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1).\end{array}

This condition is equivalent to
\kappa_{k+1}\geq 1-\frac{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{\Gamma_{k+1}t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}+\frac{5\beta}{\mu\Gamma_{k+1}}
, which is exactly the first condition in (
35
).

Using
\varphi_{k}
, we can derive from (
33
) that

\hskip-2.15277pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}&\geq&\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\mathcal{P}_{k+1}\big]+\frac{\beta}{2}\big[(2-3\mu)t_{k}+6\mu^{2}\big]\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }\frac{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\sigma_{k}^{2}+\frac{\beta t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2\mu}\Delta_{k-1}+\frac{(2\Lambda-\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k})t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\big[\bar{\beta}-\frac{(2+\mu)\beta}{2-\mu}\big]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-17.22217pt

(64)

Let us denote by
\hat{\psi}_{k}:=\beta\big[(2-3\mu)t_{k}+6\mu^{2}\big]\geq 0
.
Then, (
64
) is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\mathcal{P}_{k+1}\big]&\leq&\mathcal{P}_{k}-\frac{\hat{\psi}_{k}}{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2\mu}\Delta_{k-1}+\frac{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\sigma_{k}^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }\big[\bar{\beta}-\frac{(2+\mu)\beta}{2-\mu}\big]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }\frac{(2\Lambda-\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k})t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}.\end{array}

(65)

Since
2\Lambda-\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k}\geq 0
from the second condition of (
35
), dropping the last term of (
65
), and then taking the total expectation
\mathbb{E}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides of the result, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{k+1}\big]&\leq&\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{k}\big]-\frac{\hat{\psi}_{k}}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\big]-\frac{\beta t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2\mu}\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{k-1}\big]+\frac{t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\Gamma_{k}\sigma_{k}^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }\big[\bar{\beta}-\frac{(2+\mu)\beta}{2-\mu}\big]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

(66)

Let
\underline{B}_{K}:=\frac{1}{2\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\sigma_{k}^{2}
.
Then, since
\Gamma_{k}\leq\frac{2\Lambda}{\Theta_{k}}
by (
35
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\underline{B}_{K}:=\frac{1}{2\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{K}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\Gamma_{k}\sigma_{k}^{2}&\leq&B_{K}:=\frac{\Lambda}{\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{K}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}.\end{array}

By induction and the last relation, we obtain from (
66
) that

\hskip-0.86108pt\begin{array}[]{llcl}&\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{K}\big]&\leq&\mathcal{P}_{0}+\beta B_{K-1}\\
&\sum_{k=0}^{K}\hat{\psi}_{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&2\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{0}\big]+2\beta B_{K},\\
&\sum_{k=0}^{K}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{k-1}\big]&\leq&\frac{2\mu}{\beta}\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{0}\big]+\beta B_{K}\big),\\
&\sum_{k=0}^{K}\big[\bar{\beta}-\frac{(2+\mu)\beta}{2-\mu}\big]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{0}\big]+\beta B_{K}.\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(67)

Because
x^{0}=z^{0}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{0}&:=&\beta a_{0}\|G_{\lambda}x^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{1-\mu}{2\nu\eta_{0}}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\beta\mu(r-1)(\mu r-2\mu-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{2r-1}{4\beta(\mu r-1)}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\beta\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2},\quad\text{where}\ \ \mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}:=\mu^{2}r^{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{2r-1}{4\beta^{2}(\mu r-1)}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Since
\kappa_{0}\in(0,1]
,
x^{-1}=x^{0}
,
\Delta_{-1}=\Delta_{0}
, and
\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{0}\big]\leq\sigma_{0}^{2}
, from (
26
), we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{0}\big]&=&\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{Q}_{0}\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{L}_{0}\big]+\frac{(r-1)(\mu r-\mu-1)[(1-\kappa_{0})\mu\Gamma_{0}+\beta]}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{0}\big]\\
&\leq&\beta\big[\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}\big]+\frac{\mu r^{2}(\mu\Gamma_{0}+\beta)}{2\beta}\sigma_{0}^{2}\big]\\
&=&\beta\big(\Psi_{0}^{2}+E_{0}^{2}),\end{array}

where
\Psi_{0}^{2}:=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}\big]
and
E_{0}^{2}:=\frac{\mu r^{2}(\mu\Gamma_{0}+\beta)}{2\beta}\sigma_{0}^{2}
are given in (
37
).

Now, since
A_{k}:=\beta t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-2-2\mu)+2\bar{\beta}t_{k-1}\geq\beta[t_{k-1}^{2}-2(1+\mu)t_{k-1}+\frac{2(2+\mu)}{2-\mu}t_{k-1}]\geq\beta t_{k-1}^{2}
and
1-\mu-2\nu\geq 0
, from Lemma
10
, we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&\geq&\frac{A_{k}}{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\geq\frac{\beta t_{k-1}^{2}}{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}=\frac{\beta\mu^{2}(k+r-1)^{2}}{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Combining the last two bounds and the first estimate of (
67
), we can derive that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{\beta\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{L}_{K}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{K}\big]\leq\beta\big(\Psi_{0}^{2}+E_{0}^{2}+B_{K}\big).\end{array}

This expression leads to (
36
).

Next, since
\hat{\psi}_{k}=\beta\big[(2-3\mu)t_{k}+6\mu^{2}\big]>0
due to
0<\mu<\frac{2}{3}
, we obtain from the second estimate of (
67
) that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\beta\big[(2-3\mu)\mu(k+r)+6\mu^{2}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{0}\big]+2\beta B_{K}.\end{array}

Substituting
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{0}\big]\leq\beta(\Psi_{0}^{2}+E_{0}^{2})
into this inequality, we obtain the first line of (
38
).
The second bound of (
38
) is a consequence of the third line of (
67
) and the facts that
\mathbb{E}\big[\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{k-1}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{0}\big]\leq\beta(\Psi_{0}^{2}+E_{0}^{2})
.
Similarly, the third line of (
38
) is a direct consequence of the last line of (
67
) and
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{0}\big]\leq\beta(\Psi_{0}^{2}+E_{0}^{2})
.
∎

C.5
The proof of Theorem
13
— The
\mathcal{O}\left(1/k^{2}\right)
and
o\big(1/k^{2}\big)
-convergence rates

Proof.

Let us divide this proof into several steps as follows.

Step 1. Proving (
40
).

Since
B_{\infty}:=\frac{\Lambda}{\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}<+\infty
by (
39
), (
40
) follows directly from (
36
) and
B_{k}\leq B_{\infty}
.

Step 2. The first two summability bounds of (
41
).

By our condition (
39
), we have
\Psi_{0}^{2}+B_{K}\leq\Psi_{0}^{2}+B_{\infty}
.
Combining this fact,
e^{k}_{\lambda}:=\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}
,
e^{k}:=\widetilde{F}^{k}-Fx^{k}
and
\|e^{k}_{\lambda}\|\leq\|e^{k}\|
from (
22
), we can show from (
38
) that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|e^{k}_{\lambda}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{k+1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty.\end{array}

(68)

These prove the first and second lines of (
41
).
Here, the last line of (
68
) requires
0<\beta<\frac{(2-\mu)\bar{\beta}}{2+\mu}
.
Combining
\|\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}\leq 2\|e_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}+2\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}
and the first two lines of (
68
) we get.

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}\big]&<&+\infty.\end{array}

(69)

Step 3. The last summability bound of (
41
).

From (
VFOSA+
) we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k}(x^{k+1}-x^{k}+\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k})&=&z^{k}-x^{k},\\
(t_{k-1}-1)(x^{k}-x^{k-1}+\eta_{k-1}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1})&=&z^{k-1}-x^{k}-\eta_{k-1}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1}\\
&=&z^{k}-x^{k}-(1-\nu)\eta_{k-1}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1}.\end{array}

Combining these two expressions, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k}(x^{k+1}-x^{k}+\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k})&=&(t_{k-1}-1)(x^{k}-x^{k-1}+\eta_{k-1}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1})+(1-\nu)\eta_{k-1}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1}.\end{array}

(70)

If we denote
v^{k}:=x^{k+1}-x^{k}+\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}
, then we can rewrite the last expression as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k}v^{k}&:=&\big(1-\frac{1}{t_{k-1}}\big)t_{k-1}v^{k-1}+\frac{(1-\nu)\eta_{k-1}t_{k-1}}{t_{k-1}}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1}.\end{array}

By the convexity of
\|\cdot\|^{2}
and
\frac{1}{t_{k-1}}\in(0,1]
, since
\eta_{k}=\frac{2\beta(t_{k}-1)}{t_{k}-\nu}\leq 2\beta
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k}^{2}\|v^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&\big(1-\frac{1}{t_{k-1}}\big)t_{k-1}^{2}\|v^{k-1}\|^{2}+(1-\nu)^{2}\eta_{k-1}^{2}t_{k-1}\|\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&t_{k-1}^{2}\|v^{k-1}\|^{2}-t_{k-1}\|v^{k-1}\|^{2}+4(1-\nu)^{2}\beta^{2}t_{k-1}\|\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(71)

Taking the total expectation of this inequality, and then applying Lemma
28
with the fact that
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}\big]<+\infty
from (
69
), we conclude that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|v^{k}\|^{2}\big]\penalty\ \textrm{exists}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\|v^{k}\|^{2}\big]<+\infty.\end{array}

(72)

By Young’s inequality and
\eta_{k}\leq 2\beta
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}\leq 2\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}+\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}+2\eta_{k}^{2}\|\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}\leq 2\|v^{k}\|^{2}+8\beta^{2}\|\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Combining this inequality, (
69
), (
72
), we get the last summability bound of (
41
).

Step 4. The limit of
t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|v^{k}\|^{2}\big]
.

Since
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\|v^{k}\|^{2}\big]<+\infty
and
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|v^{k}\|^{2}\big]
exists,
applying Lemma
29
, we conclude that

\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|v^{k}\|^{2}\big]=\lim_{k\to\infty}k^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|v^{k}\|^{2}\big]=0.

(73)

Step 5. The first limit of (
42
).

From (
70
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k}[x^{k+1}-x^{k}+\eta_{k}(\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k})]&=&(t_{k-1}-1)\big[x^{k}-x^{k-1}+\eta_{k-1}(\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1})\big]\\
&&+{\penalty\ }(1-\nu)\eta_{k-1}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1}-t_{k}\eta_{k}G_{\lambda}x^{k}+(t_{k-1}-1)\eta_{k-1}G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}.\end{array}

Let us define
w^{k}:=x^{k+1}-x^{k}+\eta_{k}e_{\lambda}^{k}
for
e_{\lambda}^{k}:=\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k}
.
Then this expression becomes

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k}w^{k}&=&(t_{k-1}-1)w^{k-1}+(1-\nu)\eta_{k-1}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1}-t_{k}\eta_{k}G_{\lambda}x^{k}+(t_{k-1}-1)\eta_{k-1}G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\\
&=&\big(1-\frac{1}{t_{k-1}}\big)t_{k-1}\big[w^{k-1}-\frac{t_{k}\eta_{k}}{t_{k-1}-1}(G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1})\big]\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{1}{t_{k-1}}\left[t_{k-1}[\eta_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)-t_{k}\eta_{k}]G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}+(1-\nu)t_{k-1}\eta_{k-1}e_{\lambda}^{k-1}\right].\end{array}

By the convexity of
\|\cdot\|^{2}
, Young’s inequality, the
\bar{\beta}
-co-coercivity
of
G_{\lambda}
from (
10
), and
\|e^{k}_{\lambda}\|\leq\|e^{k}\|
from (
22
), we can deduce that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k}^{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&\left(t_{k-1}-1\right)t_{k-1}\|w^{k-1}-\frac{t_{k}\eta_{k}}{t_{k-1}-1}(G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }t_{k-1}\|[\eta_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)-t_{k}\eta_{k}]G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}+(1-\nu)\eta_{k-1}e_{\lambda}^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\left(t_{k-1}-1\right)t_{k-1}\|w^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{t_{k-1}t_{k}^{2}\eta_{k}^{2}}{t_{k-1}-1}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }2t_{k-1}t_{k}\eta_{k}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1},x^{k}-x^{k-1}\rangle-2t_{k-1}t_{k}\eta_{k-1}\eta_{k}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1},e_{\lambda}^{k-1}\rangle\\
&&+{\penalty\ }2t_{k-1}[\eta_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)-t_{k}\eta_{k}]^{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}+2(1-\nu)^{2}t_{k-1}\eta_{k-1}^{2}\|e^{k-1}_{\lambda}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\left(t_{k-1}-1\right)t_{k-1}\|w^{k-1}\|^{2}+t_{k-1}t_{k}\eta_{k}\left(\frac{t_{k}\eta_{k}}{t_{k-1}-1}-2\bar{\beta}+\eta_{k-1}\right)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }2t_{k-1}(\eta_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)-t_{k}\eta_{k})^{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }t_{k-1}\eta_{k-1}[2(1-\nu)^{2}\eta_{k-1}+t_{k}\eta_{k}]\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Let us denote by
Z_{k}:=s_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}+\hat{s}_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}+\tilde{s}_{k}\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}
, where

\begin{array}[]{lclcl}s_{k}&:=&t_{k-1}t_{k}\eta_{k}\big(\frac{t_{k}\eta_{k}}{t_{k-1}-1}-2\bar{\beta}+\eta_{k-1}\big)&\leq&8\beta^{2}t_{k}^{2},\\
\hat{s}_{k}&:=&2t_{k-1}[\eta_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)-t_{k}\eta_{k}]^{2}&\leq&8\beta^{2}(\mu+\nu)^{2}t_{k-1},\\
\tilde{s}_{k}&:=&t_{k-1}\eta_{k-1}[2(1-\nu)^{2}\eta_{k-1}+t_{k}\eta_{k}]&\leq&4\beta^{2}t_{k-1}(t_{k}+2)\leq 8\beta^{2}t_{k}^{2}.\end{array}

Then, the last expression can be briefly rewritten as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k}^{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&t_{k-1}^{2}\|w^{k-1}\|^{2}-t_{k-1}\|w^{k-1}\|^{2}+Z_{k}.\end{array}

(74)

Taking the total expectation
\mathbb{E}\big[\cdot\big]
on both sides of this inequality, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq t_{k-1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k-1}\|^{2}\big]-t_{k-1}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k-1}\|^{2}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{k}\big].\end{array}

Using the upper bound of the coefficients
s_{k}
,
\hat{s}_{k}
, and
\tilde{s}_{k}
, and (
68
), we can easily show that
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{k}\big]<+\infty
.
Applying again Lemma
28
to the last inequality, we conclude that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]\penalty\ \textrm{exists}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]<+\infty.\end{array}

Applying Lemma
29
, these statements imply that
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]=0
.

By Young’s inequality and
\|e^{k}_{\lambda}\|\leq\|e^{k}\|
, one has

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}\leq 2\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}+\eta_{k}e_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}+2\eta_{k}^{2}\|e_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}\leq 2\|w^{k}\|^{2}+8\beta^{2}\|e^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(75)

Combining this fact,
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]=0
and (
68
), we prove the second limit of (
42
).

Step 6. The second limit of (
42
).

Finally, since
\eta_{k}=\frac{2\beta(t_{k}-1)}{t_{k}-\nu}\geq\frac{4\beta(r\mu-1)}{\mu(2r-1)}
, by Young’s inequality, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{16\beta^{2}(r\mu-1)^{2}}{\mu^{2}(2r-1)^{2}}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&\eta_{k}^{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&2\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}+\eta_{k}(\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k})\|^{2}+2\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}+\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&2\|w^{k}\|^{2}+2\|v^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

This fact, (
73
), and
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w^{k}\|^{2}\big]=0
imply the first limit of (
42
).
∎

C.6
The proof of Theorem
14
— Almost sure convergence

Proof.

Again, we divide this proof into the following steps.

Step 1. The first summability bound in (
44
).

First, applying our assumption
\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k}\leq\Lambda
from (
43
), we can deduce from (
65
) that

\hskip-2.15277pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{P}_{k+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\big]&\leq&\mathcal{P}_{k}-\frac{\hat{\psi}_{k}}{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2\mu}\Delta_{k-1}-\frac{\Lambda t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}\\
&&-{\penalty\ }\big(\bar{\beta}-\frac{(2+\mu)\beta}{2-\mu}\big)t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}+\frac{\Lambda t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\sigma_{k}^{2}}{2\Theta_{k}}.\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(76)

Next, we denote
U_{k}:=\mathcal{P}_{k}
,
\gamma_{k}:=0
,
E_{k}:=\frac{\Lambda t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\sigma_{k}^{2}}{2\Theta_{k}}
, and

\begin{array}[]{lcl}V_{k}&:=&\frac{\hat{\psi}_{k}}{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\beta t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2\mu}\Delta_{k-1}+(\bar{\beta}-2\beta)t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{\Lambda t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}.\end{array}

These quantities are nonnegative.
By (
39
), we have
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}E_{k}=\frac{\beta}{2}B_{\infty}<+\infty
surely.
In addition,
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma_{k}=0<+\infty
and
\hat{\psi}_{k}=2\beta[(2-5\mu)t_{k}-(2-3\mu-2\mu^{2})]=\mathcal{O}\big(t_{k}\big)>0
surely.
Moreover,
\mathcal{F}_{k}
is a filtration.
By Lemma
30
, we conclude that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{P}_{k}\quad\textrm{exists almost surely},\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty\quad\textrm{almost surely},\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}&<&+\infty\quad\textrm{almost surely},\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\Delta_{k}&<&+\infty\quad\textrm{almost surely},\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}&<&+\infty\quad\textrm{almost surely}.\end{array}\vskip-2.15277pt

(77)

The second line of (
77
) is the first summability bound of (
44
).

Step 2. The second summability bound in (
44
).

Since
\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]
by the first line of (
12
) and
\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k}\leq\Lambda
by (
43
), utilizing these relations and the second line of (
12
), we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Gamma_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&\Gamma_{k}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\Delta_{k}\big]\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:VR_property}}{\leq}\Gamma_{k}(1-\kappa_{k})\Delta_{k-1}+\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}+\Gamma_{k}\sigma_{k}^{2}\\
&\leq&\Gamma_{k}(1-\kappa_{k})\Delta_{k-1}+\Lambda\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}+\frac{\Lambda\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}.\end{array}

Multiplying both sides of this inequality by
t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)
and utilizing
(1-\kappa_{k})\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\leq\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)
from (
32
) and
\Gamma_{k-1}\leq\frac{\Lambda}{\underline{\Theta}}
from (
43
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\|e^{k}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)\Delta_{k-1}+\Lambda t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\big(\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}+\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}\big)\\
&\leq&\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}-\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\frac{\Lambda}{\underline{\Theta}}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)\Delta_{k-1}+\Lambda t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\big(\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}+\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}\big).\end{array}

Let us denote by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}E_{k}&:=&\frac{\Lambda}{\underline{\Theta}}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)\Delta_{k-1}+\Lambda t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}+\Lambda t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}.\end{array}

Then, by (
77
) and (
39
), we have
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}E_{k}\leq\frac{\Lambda}{\underline{\Theta}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\Delta_{k}+\Lambda\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}^{2}\bar{\mathcal{E}}_{k}+\beta B_{\infty}<+\infty
almost surely.
Thus, applying again Lemma
30
with
U_{k}:=\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}
,
\gamma_{k}:=0
,
V_{k}:=\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)\|e^{k-1}\|^{2}
, and
E_{k}
given above, we conclude that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Gamma_{k}t^{2}_{k}\|e^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty\quad\textrm{almost surely}.\end{array}

However, since
\Gamma_{k}\geq\underline{\Gamma}>0
by (
43
), this implies the second summability bound in (
44
).

Step 3. The last summability bound in (
44
).

Since
\|\widetilde{G}^{k}_{\lambda}\|^{2}\leq 2\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+2\|e_{\lambda}^{k}\|^{2}\leq 2\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+2\|e^{k}\|^{2}
, using the second lines of (
77
) and (
44
), this inequality implies that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\|\widetilde{G}^{k}_{\lambda}\|^{2}<+\infty\quad\textrm{almost surely}.\end{array}

(78)

Let
v^{k}:=x^{k+1}-x^{k}+\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}
.
Taking the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[\cdot\big]
of (
71
), we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}_{k}\big[t_{k}^{2}\|v^{k}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&t_{k-1}^{2}\|v^{k-1}\|^{2}-t_{k-1}\|v^{k-1}\|^{2}+4(1-\nu)^{2}\beta^{2}t_{k-1}\|\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Since
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\|\widetilde{G}^{k}_{\lambda}\|^{2}<+\infty
by (
78
), applying Lemma
30
to the last inequality, and then using Lemma
29
, we conclude that the following statements hold almost surely:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\|v^{k}\|^{2}=0\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\|v^{k}\|^{2}<+\infty.\end{array}

(79)

Let
w^{k}:=x^{k+1}-x^{k}+\eta_{k}e_{\lambda}^{k}
as before.
Following similar arguments here, but applying them to (
74
), we can also prove that the following statements hold almost surely:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}=0\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\|w^{k}\|^{2}<+\infty.\end{array}

(80)

Combining (
75
), (
80
), and the second line of (
44
), we can easily prove that
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}t_{k}\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}<+\infty
almost surely.
This is the last line of (
44
).

Step 4. The limits of (
45
).

Combining the limits in (
79
) and (
80
) and Young’s inequality, similar to
Step 6
in the proof of Theorem
13
, we can easily show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}=0\quad\textrm{almost surely.}\end{array}

(81)

This limit is the first limit in (
45
).
The second limit of (
45
) follows from (
75
), the first limit of (
80
), and
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\|e^{k}\|^{2}=0
almost surely from (
44
).

Step 5. The existence of
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}
.

From (
VFOSA+
), we can easily shows that
z^{k}-x^{k}=t_{k}(x^{k+1}-x^{k})+t_{k}\eta_{k}\widetilde{G}_{\lambda}^{k}=t_{k}v^{k}
.
Since
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\|v^{k}\|^{2}=0
almost surely as shown in (
79
), we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|z^{k}-x^{k}\|=0
almost surely.
Using this limit and
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}=0
from (
81
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}2|t_{k}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle\leq\|x^{k}-z^{k}\|^{2}+t_{k}^{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}\to 0\quad\textrm{as}\ k\to\infty\quad\textrm{almost surely}.\end{array}

Hence, we get
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}|\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle|=0
almost surely.

Next, as discussed in Assumption
1.2
,
F
is
L
-Lipschitz continuous, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}|\langle Fx^{k}-Fx^{k-1},x^{k}-x^{k-1}\rangle|\leq\|Fx^{k}-Fx^{k-1}\|\|x^{k}-x^{k-1}\|=L\|x^{k}-x^{k-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Since
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}^{2}\|x^{k}-x^{k-1}\|^{2}=0
due to the second limit of (
45
), we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)|\langle Fx^{k}-Fx^{k-1},x^{k}-x^{k-1}\rangle|=0
almost surely.

Now, from (
26
) we can write

\vskip-2.15277pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}&=&\beta a_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}+t_{k-1}\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle+\frac{[\mu(1-\kappa_{k})\Gamma_{k}+\beta]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2\mu}\Delta_{k-1}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\big[\bar{\beta}-\frac{(2+\mu)\beta}{2-\mu}\big]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\Lambda Lt_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\langle Fx^{k}-Fx^{k-1},x^{k}-x^{k-1}\rangle+\frac{c_{k}}{2\nu\beta}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}.\end{array}\vskip-1.07639pt

(82)

Collecting all the necessary almost sure limits proven above, we have, almost surely

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{P}_{k}\ \ \textrm{exists as shown in \eqref{eq:VrAFBS4NI_th3_proof2}},\\
\lim_{k\to\infty}a_{k}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}=\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|^{2}=0\quad\textrm{by \eqref{eq:VrAFBS4NI_th3_proof5}},\\
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}|\langle G_{\lambda}x^{k},x^{k}-z^{k}\rangle|=0,\\
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)|\langle Fx^{k}-Fx^{k-1},x^{k}-x^{k-1}\rangle|=0,\\
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}-G_{\lambda}x^{k-1}\|^{2}=0\quad\textrm{from \eqref{eq:VrAFBS4NI_th3_proof2}},\\
\lim_{k\to\infty}t_{k}^{2}\Delta_{k}=0\quad\textrm{from \eqref{eq:VrAFBS4NI_th3_proof2}}.\end{array}\vskip-2.15277pt

Combining these limits and (
82
), and noting that
c_{k}=\mathcal{O}\big(1\big)
due to (
27
), we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}
exists almost surely.

Step 6. Almost sure convergence of
\{x^{k}\}
and
\{z^{k}\}
.

Since
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|x^{k}-z^{k}\|=0
and
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\|
exists almost surely, combining these facts and
|\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|-\|z^{k}-x^{\star}\||\leq\|x^{k}-z^{k}\|
, we conclude that
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}
exists almost surely.

Finally, since
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}
exists almost surely,
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|G_{\lambda}x^{k}\|=0
almost surely by the second line of (
77
), and
G_{\lambda}
is continuous, applying Lemma
31
, we conclude that
\{x^{k}\}
converges to a random variable
\bar{x}^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(G_{\lambda})
almost surely.
However, since
\bar{x}^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(G_{\lambda})
if and only if
\bar{x}^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
surely, we also conclude that
\{x^{k}\}
almost surely converges to a solution
\bar{x}^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
.
In addition, since
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|z^{k}-x^{k}\|=0
almost surely, we can state that
\{z^{k}\}
almost surely converges to
\bar{x}^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
.
∎

Appendix D
The Proof of Corollaries in Subsections
4.4
and
4.5

We now present the full proofs of Corollaries in Subsections
4.4
and
4.5
.

D.1
The finite-sum setting (

F

)

This appendix presents the proof of Corollaries
18
,
19
, and
20
, respectively.

D.1.1
The proof of Corollary
18
— The L-SVRG variant of
VFOSA+

Proof.

Since the
L-SVRG
estimator is used and
\bar{F}\tilde{x}^{k}:=F\tilde{x}^{k}
, by Lemma
5
, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\Delta_{k}=\hat{\Delta}_{k},\quad\kappa_{k}=\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k},\quad\Theta_{k}=\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)b_{k}\mathbf{p}_{k}}\geq\underline{\Theta}:=\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)n}>0,\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sigma_{k}^{2}=0.\end{array}

Thus, the quantity
B_{K}
in Theorem
12
becomes
B_{K}:=\frac{\Lambda}{\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{K}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}=0
.
Moreover, since
x^{0}=\tilde{x}^{0}
, we also have
\Delta_{0}=\hat{\Delta}_{0}:=\frac{1}{nb_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{0}-F_{i}\tilde{x}^{0}\|^{2}\leq\sigma_{0}^{2}=0
.

Next, let us choose
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}
and assume that
\Gamma_{k}=\frac{5c_{p}\beta n^{\omega}}{\mu}=:\underline{\Gamma}>0
for all
k\geq 0
and given
c_{p}>0
and
\omega>0
.
We recall the first condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\kappa_{k}&=&\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k}=\frac{\mathbf{p}_{k}}{2}\geq 1-\frac{\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)}{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}+\frac{5\beta}{\mu\Gamma_{k}}=1+\frac{5\beta}{\mu\Gamma_{k}}-\frac{t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)}{t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}\\
&=&\frac{1}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}+\frac{2\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}-\frac{\mu+1}{(k+r-1)(\mu(k+r-1)-1)}.\end{array}

This condition holds if
\mathbf{p}_{k}\geq\frac{2}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}+\frac{4\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}
.

Now, for
r>5+\frac{1}{\mu}
and
n^{\omega}\geq\frac{1}{c_{p}}\max\big\{\frac{2\mu(r-1)-2}{\mu(r-5)-1},\frac{\mu(r-1)-1}{4\mu}\big\}
, if we choose

\mathbf{p}_{k}:=\begin{cases}\frac{2}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}+\frac{4\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}&\textrm{if}\penalty\ 0\leq k\leq K_{0}:=\lfloor 4c_{p}n^{\omega}+1+\mu^{-1}-r\rfloor,\\
\frac{3}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}&\textrm{otherwise},\end{cases}\vskip-1.07639pt

then
0<\frac{2}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}+\frac{4\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}\leq\mathbf{p}_{k}\leq 1
.
Consequently, the first condition in (
35
) holds.

Since
\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k}\leq\frac{10c_{p}\beta n^{\omega}}{\mu b_{k}\mathbf{p}_{k}}\leq\frac{5\beta c_{p}^{2}n^{2\omega}}{\mu b_{k}}
, the second condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
and (
43
) in Theorem
14
both hold if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{5\beta c_{p}^{2}n^{2\omega}}{\mu b}\leq\Lambda.\end{array}

Clearly, if we choose
b_{k}=b:=\big\lfloor\frac{5\beta c_{p}^{2}n^{2\omega}}{\mu\Lambda}\big\rfloor=\lfloor c_{b}n^{2\omega}\rfloor
with
c_{b}:=\frac{5\beta c_{p}^{2}}{\mu\Lambda}
, then this condition holds.
Consequently, the second condition of (
35
) and (
43
) hold.

Since
B_{K}=0
and
E_{0}^{2}=0
, for a given
\epsilon>0
, to guarantee
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
, from (
36
) we can impose that
\frac{2\Psi_{0}^{2}}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
The last condition holds if we choose
K:=\big\lfloor\frac{\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}\big\rfloor-1
.

The expected total number of oracle calls is
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]:=n+\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]
, where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]&=&\sum_{k=0}^{K}(n\mathbf{p}_{k}+2b)\\
&=&n\sum_{k=0}^{K}\mathbf{p}_{k}+2b(K+1)\\
&=&2b(K+1)+n\sum_{k=0}^{K_{0}}\mathbf{p}_{k}+n\sum_{k=K_{0}+1}^{K}\mathbf{p}_{k}\\
&\leq&2b(K+1)+n\big[\sum_{k=0}^{K_{0}}\frac{2}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}+4\sum_{k=0}^{K_{0}}\frac{\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}+\sum_{k=K_{0}+1}^{K}\frac{3}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{2\sqrt{2}c_{b}\Psi_{0}n^{2\omega}}{\mu\epsilon}+n\big[\frac{2(4c_{p}n^{\omega}-r+1+\mu^{-1})}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}+4\ln(4c_{p}n^{\omega}-r+2+\mu^{-1})+\frac{3\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{c_{p}n^{\omega}\mu\epsilon}\big]\\
&\leq&4n\big[2+\ln(4c_{p}n^{\omega})\big]+\frac{\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}\big(2c_{b}n^{2\omega}+\frac{3n^{1-\omega}}{c_{p}}\big).\end{array}

Here, we have used
r-1-\frac{1}{\mu}\geq 1
and
\sum_{k=0}^{K_{0}}\frac{\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}\leq\ln(1+(r-1-\frac{1}{\mu})^{-1}K_{0})\leq\ln(K_{0}+1)
in the last inequality.
Therefore, we conclude that the expected total number of oracle calls
F_{i}
and evaluations
J_{\lambda T}
in (
VFOSA+
) is at most

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]&:=&\Big\lfloor n+4n\big[2+\ln(4c_{p}n^{\omega})\big]+\frac{\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}\big(2c_{b}n^{2\omega}+\frac{3n^{1-\omega}}{c_{p}}\big)\Big\rfloor\end{array}

to achieve
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
In particular, if we choose
\omega:=\frac{1}{3}
, then we get
