\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]:=\big\lfloor n+4n\big[2+\ln(4c_{p}n^{1/3})\big]+\frac{\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}(3+2c_{b}c_{p})n^{2/3}}{c_{p}\mu\epsilon}\big\rfloor
.
∎

D.1.2
The proof of Corollary
19
— The SAGA variant of
VFOSA+

Proof.

Since the
SAGA
estimator is used, by Lemma
6
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\kappa_{k}=\frac{\alpha b_{k}}{n},\quad\Theta_{k}=\frac{(3-\alpha)n}{(1-\alpha)b_{k}^{2}}\geq\underline{\Theta}:=\frac{3-\alpha}{(1-\alpha)n}>0,\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sigma_{k}^{2}=0.\end{array}

Note that
B_{K}
in Theorem
12
becomes
B_{K}:=\frac{\Lambda}{\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{K}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}=0
.
Moreover, since
\hat{F}_{i}^{0}=F_{i}x^{0}
for all
i\in[n]
, we also have
\Delta_{0}:=\frac{1}{nb_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|F_{i}x^{0}-\hat{F}_{i}^{0}\|^{2}\leq\sigma_{0}^{2}=0
.

Next, let us choose
\alpha:=\frac{1}{2}
and
\Gamma_{k}:=\frac{5\beta n^{\omega}}{\mu c_{b}}=:\underline{\Gamma}>0
for all
k\geq 0
and given
\omega>0
and
c_{b}>0
.
We recall the first condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\kappa_{k}=\frac{\alpha b_{k}}{n}=\frac{b_{k}}{2n}\geq 1-\frac{\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)}{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}+\frac{5\beta}{\mu\Gamma_{k}}=\frac{c_{b}}{n^{\omega}}+\frac{2\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}-\frac{1+\mu}{(k+r-1)(\mu(k+r-1)-1)}.\end{array}

Let us choose

b_{k}:=\begin{cases}2c_{b}n^{1-\omega}+\frac{4\mu n}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}&\textrm{if}\penalty\ \penalty\ k\leq K_{0}:=\lfloor 4n^{\omega}+1+\mu^{-1}-r\rfloor,\\
3c_{b}n^{1-\omega},&\textrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then, the last condition holds.
Consequently, the first condition of (
35
) holds.
Clearly, we have
b_{k}\leq b_{k-1}
for all
k\geq 1
.
Moreover, using the update of
b_{k}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}b_{k-1}-b_{k}=\frac{4\mu^{2}n}{[\mu(k+r-1)-1][\mu(k+r-2)-1]}\leq\frac{b_{k}b_{k-1}}{4n}.\end{array}

Therefore, we finally get
b_{k-1}-\frac{(1-\alpha)b_{k}b_{k-1}}{2n}\leq b_{k}\leq b_{k-1}
, which verifies the condition of Lemma
6
.
Note that since
1\leq b_{k}\leq n
and
K_{0}\geq 0
, we require
r>5+\frac{1}{\mu}
and
n^{\omega}\geq\max\big\{\frac{\mu(r-1)-1}{4\mu},\frac{2c_{b}[\mu(r-1)-1]}{\mu(r-5)-1}\big\}
as stated in Corollary
19
.

From the definition of
\Theta_{k}
above and
b_{k}\geq 2c_{b}n^{1-\omega}
, we can easily show that
\Theta_{k}=\frac{(3-\alpha)n}{(1-\alpha)b_{k}^{2}}=\frac{5n}{b_{k}^{2}}\leq\frac{5}{4c_{b}^{2}n^{1-2\omega}}
.
Since
\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k}\leq\frac{25\beta n^{\omega}}{4\mu c_{b}^{2}n^{1-2\omega}}
, the second condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
and the condition (
43
) in Theorem
14
both hold if
\frac{25\beta}{4\mu c_{b}^{2}n^{1-3\omega}}\leq\Lambda
.
Let us choose
\omega:=\frac{1}{3}
and
c_{b}:=\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{\mu\Lambda}}
.
Then, the last condition holds.
Consequently, the second condition of (
35
) and (
43
) are both satisfied.

Since
B_{K}=0
and
E_{0}^{2}=0
, for a given
\epsilon>0
, to guarantee
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
, from (
36
), we can impose
\frac{2\Psi_{0}^{2}}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
This condition holds if we choose
K:=\big\lfloor\frac{\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}\big\rfloor-1
.

The expected total number of oracle calls becomes
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]:=n+\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]
, where
n
is the number of
F_{i}
evaluations for the first epoch, and

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]&=&\sum_{k=0}^{K}b_{k}=\sum_{k=0}^{K_{0}}b_{k}+\sum_{k=K_{0}+1}^{K}b_{k}\\
&\leq&2c_{b}n^{2/3}(K_{0}+1)+\sum_{k=0}^{K_{0}}\frac{4\mu n}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}+3c_{b}(K-K_{0})n^{2/3}\\
&\leq&8c_{b}n^{2/3}n^{1/3}+4n\ln(K_{0}+1)+3c_{b}Kn^{2/3}\\
&\leq&8c_{b}n+4n\ln(4n^{1/3})+\frac{3\sqrt{2}c_{b}\Psi_{0}n^{2/3}}{\mu\epsilon}.\end{array}

Thus, we get
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]:=\big\lfloor[8c_{b}+4\ln(4n^{1/3})]n+\frac{3\sqrt{2}c_{b}\Psi_{0}n^{2/3}}{\mu\epsilon}\big\rfloor
.
We conclude that the total number of
F_{i}
and
J_{\lambda T}
evaluations of (
VFOSA+
) is at most
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]:=\mathcal{O}\big(n\ln(n)+\frac{n^{2/3}}{\epsilon}\big)
to achieve
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
∎

D.1.3
The proof of Corollary
20
— The L-SARAH variant of
VFOSA+

Proof.

Since the
L-SARAH
estimator is used and
\bar{F}x^{k}=Fx^{k}
, by Lemma
7
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\kappa_{k}=\mathbf{p}_{k},\quad\Theta_{k}=\frac{1}{b_{k}}\geq\underline{\Theta}:=\frac{1}{n}>0,\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sigma_{k}^{2}=0.\end{array}

The quantity
B_{K}
in Theorem
12
becomes
B_{K}:=\frac{\Lambda}{\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{K}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}=0
.
Moreover, since
\widetilde{F}^{0}:=Fx^{0}
, we have
\|\widetilde{F}^{0}-Fx^{0}\|^{2}\leq\sigma_{0}^{2}=0
.

Next, let us choose
\Gamma_{k}=\frac{5c_{p}\beta n^{\omega}}{\mu}=:\underline{\Gamma}>0
for all
k\geq 0
and given
c_{p}>0
and
\omega>0
.
We recall the first condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\kappa_{k}=\mathbf{p}_{k}\geq 1-\frac{\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)}{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}+\frac{5\beta}{\mu\Gamma_{k}}=\frac{1}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}+\frac{2\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}-\frac{1+\mu}{(k+r-1)(\mu(k+r-1)-1)}.\end{array}

If
r>3+\frac{1}{\mu}
and
n^{\omega}\geq\frac{1}{c_{p}}\max\{\frac{\mu(r-1)-1}{\mu(r-3)-1},\frac{\mu(r-1)-1}{2\mu}\}
, then by choosing

\mathbf{p}_{k}:=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}+\frac{2\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}&\textrm{if}\penalty\ k\leq K_{0}:=\lfloor 2c_{p}n^{\omega}-r+1+\mu^{-1}\rfloor,\\
\frac{2}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}&\textrm{otherwise},\end{cases}

we have
0<\mathbf{p}_{k}\leq 1
and the last condition holds.
Thus the first condition of (
35
) holds.

Since
\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k}=\frac{5\beta c_{p}n^{\omega}}{\mu b_{k}}
,
the second condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
and the condition (
43
) in Theorem
14
both hold if
\frac{5c_{p}\beta n^{\omega}}{\mu b_{k}}\leq\Lambda
.
Clearly, if we choose
b_{k}=b:=\big\lfloor\frac{5\beta c_{p}n^{\omega}}{\mu\Lambda}\big\rfloor=\lfloor c_{b}n^{\omega}\rfloor
with
c_{b}:=\frac{5\beta c_{p}}{\mu\Lambda}
, then this condition holds.
Consequently, the second condition of (
35
) and (
43
) are both satisfied.

Since
B_{K}=0
and
E_{0}^{2}=0
, for a given
\epsilon>0
, to guarantee
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
, from (
36
), we can impose
\frac{2\Psi_{0}^{2}}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
This condition holds if we choose
K:=\big\lfloor\frac{\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}\big\rfloor-1
.

We can estimate the expected total number of oracle calls as
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]:=n+\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]
, where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]&=&\sum_{k=0}^{K}[\mathbf{p}_{k}n+2(1-\mathbf{p}_{k})b]\\
&=&n\sum_{k=0}^{K}\mathbf{p}_{k}+2b\sum_{k=0}^{K}(1-\mathbf{p}_{k})\\
&=&2b(K+1)+(n-2b)\sum_{k=0}^{K_{0}}\mathbf{p}_{k}+(n-2b)\sum_{k=K_{0}+1}^{K}\mathbf{p}_{k}\\
&\leq&2b(K+1)+n\big[\sum_{k=0}^{K_{0}}\frac{1}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}+2\sum_{k=0}^{K_{0}}\frac{\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}+\sum_{k=K_{0}+1}^{K}\frac{2}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{\sqrt{2}c_{b}\Psi_{0}n^{\omega}}{\mu\epsilon}+n\big[\frac{2c_{p}n^{\omega}-r+1+\mu^{-1}}{c_{p}n^{\omega}}+2\ln(2c_{p}n^{\omega}-r+1+\mu^{-1})+\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{c_{p}n^{\omega}\mu\epsilon}\big]\\
&\leq&2n\big[2+\ln(c_{p}n^{\omega})\big]+\frac{\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}\big(c_{b}n^{\omega}+\frac{2n^{1-\omega}}{c_{p}}\big).\end{array}

Here, we have used
r-1-\frac{1}{n}\geq 1
in the last inequality.
Hence, we conclude that the expected total number of oracle calls
F_{i}
and
J_{\lambda T}
evaluations of (
VFOSA+
) is at most

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]:=\Big\lfloor n+2n\big[2+\ln(c_{p}n^{\omega})\big]+\frac{\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}\big(c_{b}n^{\omega}+\frac{2n^{1-\omega}}{c_{p}}\big)\Big\rfloor\end{array}

to achieve
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
Clearly, if we choose
\omega=\frac{1}{2}
, then we obtain
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]:=\mathcal{O}\big(n\ln(n^{1/2})+\frac{n^{1/2}}{\epsilon}\big)
.
∎

D.2
The expectation setting (

E

)

This appendix presents the full proof of Corollaries
22
,
23
, and
24
, respectively.

D.2.1
The proof of Corollary
22
— The L-SVRG variant of
VFOSA+

Proof.

Since
L-SVRG
is used, substituting
\tau:=1
and
\alpha:=\frac{1}{2}
into Lemma
5
, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\Delta_{k}:=2\hat{\Delta}_{k}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{n_{k}},\quad\kappa_{k}:=\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k}=\frac{\mathbf{p}_{k}}{2},\quad\Theta_{k}:=\frac{2}{(1-\alpha)b_{k}\mathbf{p}_{k}}=\frac{4}{b_{k}\mathbf{p}_{k}},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sigma_{k}^{2}:=\frac{2\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k}\sigma^{2}}{n_{k}}=\frac{\mathbf{p}_{k}\sigma^{2}}{n_{k}},\end{array}

to fulfill the
\textbf{VR}(\kappa_{k},\Theta_{k},\Delta_{k},\sigma_{k})
condition of Definition
4
.

Next, let us choose
\Gamma_{k}:=\frac{5\beta}{\mu\epsilon^{\omega}}=:\underline{\Gamma}>0
for a given tolerance
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and a given
\omega>0
.
We recall the first condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\kappa_{k}=\alpha\mathbf{p}_{k}=\frac{\mathbf{p}_{k}}{2}\geq 1-\frac{\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)}{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}+\frac{5\beta}{\mu\Gamma_{k}}=\epsilon^{\omega}+\frac{2\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}-\frac{1+\mu}{(k+r-1)(\mu(k+r-1)-1)}.\end{array}

From this relation, we can see that if we choose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbf{p}_{k}&:=&2\epsilon^{\omega}+\frac{4\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}\geq\underline{\mathbf{p}}:=2\epsilon^{\omega},\end{array}

then the first condition in (
35
) holds and
\mathbf{p}_{k}\leq 1
, provided that
r>5+\frac{1}{\mu}
and
\epsilon^{\omega}\leq\frac{\mu(r-5)-1}{2\mu(r-1)-2}
.

Since
\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k}\leq\frac{10\beta}{\mu(1-\alpha)b\mathbf{p}_{k}\epsilon^{\omega}}\leq\frac{10\beta}{\mu b\epsilon^{2\omega}}
, the second condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
and the condition (
43
) in Theorem
14
both hold if
\frac{10\beta}{\mu b\epsilon^{2\omega}}\leq\Lambda
.
Therefore, if we choose
b_{k}=b:=\big\lfloor\frac{10\beta}{\mu\Lambda\epsilon^{2\omega}}\big\rfloor=\lfloor\frac{c_{b}}{\epsilon^{2\omega}}\rfloor
with
c_{b}:=\frac{10\beta}{\mu\Lambda}
, then the last condition holds.
Consequently, the second condition of (
35
) and (
43
) are both satisfied.
Moreover, since
b_{k}=b>0
for all
k\geq 0
, we have
\Theta_{k}\geq\frac{4}{b}=:\underline{\Theta}>0
, which fulfills (
43
).

If we fix
n_{k}=n>0
, then the quantity
B_{K}
in Theorem
12
becomes

\begin{array}[]{lcl}B_{K}&:=&\frac{\Lambda}{\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{K}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}=\frac{5\sigma^{2}}{\mu n\epsilon^{2\omega}}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\mathbf{p}_{k}^{2}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\\
&\leq&\frac{10\sigma^{2}}{n\epsilon^{\omega}}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\big(\epsilon^{\omega}+\frac{2\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}\big)(k+r-1)[\mu(k+r-1)-1]\\
&\leq&\frac{10\mu\sigma^{2}}{n\epsilon^{\omega}}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\big[\epsilon^{\omega}(k+r-1)^{2}+2(k+r-1)\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{10\mu\sigma^{2}}{n}\big(K+\epsilon^{-\omega}\big)(K+r-1)^{2}.\end{array}

Moreover, from the construction (
L-SVRG
) of
\widetilde{F}^{0}
, we have
E_{0}^{2}=\frac{\mu r^{2}(\mu\Gamma_{0}+\beta)\sigma^{2}}{2\beta n}
.
We also have
\Gamma_{0}\mu+\beta=\frac{5\beta}{\epsilon^{\omega}}+\beta\leq\frac{6\beta}{\epsilon^{\omega}}
.
Using these bounds, to guarantee
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
, from (
36
), we can impose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&\frac{2(\Psi_{0}^{2}+B_{K-1})}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}+\frac{\mu r^{2}(\Gamma_{0}\mu+\beta)}{\mu^{2}n\beta(K+r-1)^{2}}\sigma^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{2\Psi_{0}^{2}}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}+\frac{6r^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\mu(K+r-1)^{2}\epsilon^{\omega}n}+\frac{3\sigma^{2}}{\mu n}\big(K+\epsilon^{-\omega}\big)\\
&\leq&\epsilon^{2}.\end{array}

This condition holds if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{2\Psi_{0}^{2}}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4},\quad\frac{3\sigma^{2}K}{\mu n}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4},\quad\frac{3\sigma^{2}}{n\epsilon^{\omega}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\frac{6r^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\mu(K+r-1)^{2}\epsilon^{\omega}n}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4}.\end{array}

These four conditions are simultaneously satisfied if we choose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}K:=\big\lfloor\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}-1\big\rfloor\quad\textrm{and}\quad n\geq\sigma^{2}\max\left\{\frac{3\mu r^{2}}{\Psi_{0}^{2}\epsilon^{\omega}},\frac{12}{\epsilon^{2+\omega}},\frac{24\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu^{2}\epsilon^{3}}\right\}.\end{array}

Hence, we can choose
\omega:=1
and
n:=\frac{c_{n}}{\epsilon^{3}}=\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\big)
for given
c_{n}:=12\sigma^{2}\max\{1,\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu^{2}}\}
.

Since we fix
n_{k}=n>0
and
b_{k}=b>0
for all
k\geq 0
, the expected total number of oracle calls becomes
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]=n+\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]
, where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]&=&\sum_{k=0}^{K}(n\mathbf{p}_{k}+2b)=2b(K+1)+n\sum_{k=0}^{K}\mathbf{p}_{k}\\
&\leq&2b(K+1)+2n\big[\sum_{k=0}^{K}\epsilon+2\sum_{k=0}^{K}\frac{\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{4c_{b}\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{2c_{n}}{\epsilon^{3}}\big[\epsilon(K+1)+2\ln(K+1)\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{4\sqrt{2}c_{b}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{2c_{n}}{\epsilon^{3}}\big[\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu}+2\ln\big(\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}\big)\big].\end{array}

Therefore, we obtain
\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]=\mathcal{O}\big(\epsilon^{-3}+\epsilon^{-3}\ln(\epsilon^{-1})\big)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-3})
.
This inequality shows that the expected total number of oracle calls
\mathbf{F}(\cdot,\xi)
and
J_{\lambda T}
evaluations of (
VFOSA+
) is at most
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]:=\mathcal{O}\big(\epsilon^{-3}+\epsilon^{-3}\ln(\epsilon^{-1})\big)
to achieve
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
∎

D.2.2
The proof of Corollary
23
— The L-SARAH variant of
VFOSA+

Proof.

Since the
L-SARAH
estimator is used, by Lemma
7
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\kappa_{k}:=\mathbf{p}_{k},\quad\Theta_{k}:=\frac{1}{b_{k}},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sigma_{k}^{2}:=\frac{\mathbf{p}_{k}\sigma^{2}}{n}.\end{array}\vskip-2.15277pt

Next, let us choose
\Gamma_{k}=\frac{5\beta}{\mu\epsilon^{\omega}}=:\underline{\Gamma}>0
for all
k\geq 0
, a given tolerance
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and
\omega>0
.
We recall the first condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\kappa_{k}&=&\mathbf{p}_{k}\geq 1-\frac{\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)}{\Gamma_{k}t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}+\frac{5\beta}{\mu\Gamma_{k}}=\frac{5\beta}{\mu\Gamma_{k}}+\frac{2\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}-\frac{1+\mu}{(k+r-1)(\mu(k+r-1)-1)}\\
&=&\epsilon^{\omega}+\frac{2\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}-\frac{1+\mu}{(k+r-1)(\mu(k+r-1)-1)}.\end{array}\vskip-2.15277pt

This condition holds if we choose
\mathbf{p}_{k+1}:=\epsilon^{\omega}+\frac{2\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}
, provided that
r>3+\frac{1}{\mu}
and
0<\epsilon^{\omega}\leq\frac{\mu(r-3)-1}{\mu(r-1)-1}
.
In addition, we also have
0<\underline{\mathbf{p}}:=\epsilon^{\omega}\leq\mathbf{p}_{k}\leq 1
.

Since
\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k}=\frac{5\beta}{\mu b_{k}\epsilon^{\omega}}
, the second condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
and the condition (
43
) in Theorem
14
both hold if
\frac{5\beta}{\mu b_{k}\epsilon^{\omega}}\leq\Lambda
.
Therefore, if we choose
b_{k}=b:=\big\lfloor\frac{5\beta}{\mu\Lambda\epsilon^{\omega}}\big\rfloor=\big\lfloor\frac{c_{b}}{\epsilon^{\omega}}\big\rfloor
with
c_{b}:=\frac{5\beta}{\mu\Lambda}
, then the last condition holds.
Consequently, the second condition of (
35
) and (
43
) are both satisfied.
Since
b_{k}=b>0
, we have
\Theta_{k}\geq\frac{1}{b}=:\underline{\Theta}>0
.

If we fix
n_{k}=n>0
, then the quantity
B_{K}
in Theorem
12
becomes

\begin{array}[]{lcl}B_{K}&:=&\frac{\Lambda}{\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{K}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}=\frac{5\sigma^{2}}{2\mu n\epsilon^{\omega}}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\mathbf{p}_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\\
&=&\frac{5\sigma^{2}}{2n\epsilon^{\omega}}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\big(\epsilon^{\omega}+\frac{2\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}\big)(k+r-1)[\mu(k+r-1)-1]\\
&\leq&\frac{5\mu\sigma^{2}}{2n}\big(K+2\epsilon^{-\omega}\big)(K+r-1)^{2}.\end{array}

Moreover, we also have
E_{0}^{2}=\frac{\mu r^{2}(\mu\Gamma_{0}+\beta)\sigma^{2}}{2\beta n}
and
\Gamma_{0}\mu+\beta=\frac{5\beta}{\epsilon^{\omega}}+\beta\leq\frac{6\beta}{\epsilon^{\omega}}
.
Using these bounds, from (
36
), to guarantee
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
, we impose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&\frac{2(\Psi_{0}^{2}+B_{K-1})}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}+\frac{\mu r^{2}(\Gamma_{0}\mu+\beta)}{\mu^{2}n\beta(K+r-1)^{2}}\sigma^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{2\Psi_{0}^{2}}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}+\frac{3\sigma^{2}}{\mu n}\big(K+2\epsilon^{-\omega}\big)+\frac{6r^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\mu(K+r-1)^{2}\epsilon^{\omega}n}\\
&\leq&\epsilon^{2}.\end{array}

This condition holds if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{2\Psi_{0}^{2}}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4},\quad\frac{3\sigma^{2}K}{\mu n}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4},\quad\frac{6\sigma^{2}}{\mu\beta n\epsilon^{\omega}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\frac{6r^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\mu(K+r-1)^{2}\epsilon^{\omega}n}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4}.\end{array}

These four conditions are simultaneously satisfied if we choose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}K:=\big\lfloor\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}-1\big\rfloor\quad\textrm{and}\quad n\geq\sigma^{2}\max\left\{\frac{24\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu^{2}\epsilon^{3}},\frac{24}{\mu\beta\epsilon^{2+\omega}},\frac{3r^{2}\mu}{\Psi_{0}^{2}\epsilon^{\omega}}\right\}.\end{array}

Thus, we can choose
\omega=1
and
n:=\frac{c_{n}}{\epsilon^{3}}=\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\big)
for given
c_{n}:=24\sigma^{2}\max\left\{\frac{\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu^{2}},\frac{1}{\mu\beta}\right\}
.

Since we fix
n_{k}=n>0
and
b_{k}=b>0
, the expected total number of oracle calls becomes
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]=n+\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]
, where

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]&=&\sum_{k=0}^{K}[n\mathbf{p}_{k}+2b(1-\mathbf{p}_{k})]=n\sum_{k=0}^{K}\mathbf{p}_{k}+2b\sum_{k=0}^{K}(1-\mathbf{p}_{k})\\
&=&2b(K+1)+(n-2b)\sum_{k=0}^{K}\mathbf{p}_{k}\\
&\leq&2b(K+1)+n\big[\sum_{k=0}^{K}\epsilon+2\sum_{k=0}^{K}\frac{\mu}{\mu(k+r-1)-1}\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{4c_{b}\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{c_{n}}{\epsilon^{3}}\big[\epsilon(K+1)+2\ln(K+r)\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{4c_{b}\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{c_{n}}{\epsilon^{3}}\big[\frac{2\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu}+2\ln\big(\frac{3\sqrt{2}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}\big)\big].\end{array}

Therefore, we obtain
\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]=\mathcal{O}\big(\epsilon^{-2}+\epsilon^{-3}+\epsilon^{-3}\ln(\epsilon^{-1})\big)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-3})
.
This inequality shows that the expected total number of oracle calls
\mathbf{F}(\cdot,\xi)
and
J_{\lambda T}
evaluations of (
VFOSA+
) is at most
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]:=\mathcal{O}\big(\epsilon^{-2}+\epsilon^{-3}+\epsilon^{-3}\ln(\epsilon^{-1})\big)
to achieve
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
∎

D.2.3
The proof of Corollary
24
— The HSGD variant of
VFOSA+

Proof.

Since (
HSGD
) is used for
Fx^{k}
, by Lemma
8
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\kappa_{k}:=1-(1-\tau_{k})^{2},\quad\Theta_{k}:=\frac{2(1-\tau_{k})^{2}}{b_{k}},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\sigma_{k}^{2}:=\frac{2\tau_{k}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\hat{b}_{k}}.\end{array}

Now, let us choose
\Gamma_{k}=\frac{5\beta t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}{\theta\mu t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}\geq\frac{5\beta}{\theta\mu}=:\underline{\Gamma}>0
for all
k\geq 0
and some
\theta\in(0,1)
.
Then, the first condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\kappa_{k}=1-(1-\tau_{k})^{2}&\geq&1-\frac{\Gamma_{k-1}t_{k-2}(t_{k-2}-1)}{\Gamma_{k}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}+\frac{5\beta}{\mu\Gamma_{k}}=1-\frac{(1-\theta)t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}.\end{array}

This condition is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{(1-\theta)t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}\geq(1-\tau_{k})^{2}.\end{array}

If we choose
\tau_{k}:=1-\sqrt{\frac{(1-\theta)t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}}
as stated in Corollary
24
, then the last condition holds.
Consequently, the first condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
holds.

Since
\Theta_{k}=\frac{2(1-\tau_{k})^{2}}{b_{k}}=\frac{2(1-\theta)t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{b_{k}t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}
, we have
\Gamma_{k}\Theta_{k}=\frac{10\beta(1-\theta)}{\mu\theta b_{k}}
.
The second condition of (
35
) in Theorem
12
and the condition (
43
) in Theorem
14
both hold if
\frac{10\beta(1-\theta)}{\mu\theta b_{k}}\leq\Lambda
.
Therefore, if we choose
b_{k}=b\geq\frac{10\beta}{\mu\Lambda\theta}
, then the last condition holds.
Consequently, the second condition of (
35
) and (
43
) are both satisfied.
Moreover, since
\tau_{k}\geq 1-\sqrt{1-\theta}
,
b_{k}=b>0
and
\hat{b}_{k}=\hat{b}>0
for all
k\geq 0
, we have
\Theta_{k}\geq\underline{\Theta}:=\frac{\hat{c}(1-\theta)}{b}>0
for a given
\hat{c}>0
.

Now, since
t_{k}=t_{k-1}+\mu
, if
t_{k-1}\geq\frac{1}{1-\mu^{2}}
(which holds if
r\geq 1+\frac{1}{\mu(1-\mu^{2})}
), then we have
\sqrt{t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}\leq\sqrt{t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}+1
.
Therefore, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[1]}&:=&\big[\sqrt{t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}-\sqrt{(1-\theta)t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}\big]^{2}\\
&\leq&\big[(1-\sqrt{1-\theta})\sqrt{t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}+1\big]^{2}\\
&\leq&\big(\theta t_{k-1}+1\big)^{2}=\mu^{2}\theta^{2}(k+r-1)^{2}+2\mu\theta(k+r-1)+1.\end{array}

Since we choose
r\geq 5+\frac{1}{\mu}
, we also have
r\geq 1+\frac{1}{\mu(1-\mu^{2})}
and
\Gamma_{0}\leq\frac{5c_{0}\beta}{\mu\theta}
for a constant
c_{0}>0
.

In this case, we can bound the quantity
B_{K}
in Theorem
12
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}B_{K}&:=&\frac{\Lambda}{\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{K}t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\frac{\sigma_{k}^{2}}{\Theta_{k}}\\
&=&\frac{\Lambda\sigma^{2}b}{\beta(1-\theta)\hat{b}}\sum_{k=0}^{K}t_{k}(t_{k}-1)\Big(1-\sqrt{\frac{(1-\theta)t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}}\Big)^{2}\\
&=&\frac{\Lambda\sigma^{2}b}{\beta(1-\theta)\hat{b}}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\big[\sqrt{t_{k}(t_{k}-1)}-\sqrt{(1-\theta)t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}\big]^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{\Lambda\sigma^{2}b}{\beta(1-\theta)\hat{b}}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\big[\mu^{2}\theta^{2}(k+r-1)^{2}+2\mu\theta(k+r-1)+1\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{\Lambda\sigma^{2}b}{\beta(1-\theta)\hat{b}}\big[\mu^{2}\theta^{2}K(K+r-1)^{2}+2\mu\theta(K+r-1)^{2}+(K+1)\big].\end{array}

In addition, we also have
E_{0}^{2}=\frac{\mu r^{2}(\mu\Gamma_{0}+\beta)\sigma^{2}}{2\beta n_{0}}
.
Using these bounds and
\Gamma_{0}\leq\frac{5c_{0}\beta}{\mu\theta}
, to guarantee
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
for any
\epsilon\in(0,1/2]
, from (
36
), we impose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&\frac{2(\Psi_{0}^{2}+B_{K-1})}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}+\frac{\mu r^{2}(\Gamma_{0}\mu+\beta)}{\mu^{2}n_{0}\beta(K+r-1)^{2}}\sigma^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{2\Psi_{0}^{2}}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}+\frac{2\Lambda\sigma^{2}b}{\mu^{2}\beta(1-\theta)\hat{b}}\big(\mu^{2}\theta^{2}K+2\mu\theta+\frac{1}{K}\big)+\frac{r^{2}\sigma^{2}(5c_{0}+\theta)}{(K+r-1)^{2}\mu\theta n_{0}}\\
&\leq&\epsilon^{2}.\end{array}

If we assume that
\theta\in(0,1/2]
and
\theta\leq c_{0}
, then the last condition holds if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{2\Psi_{0}^{2}}{\mu^{2}(K+r-1)^{2}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{5},\ \ \frac{4\Lambda\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}bK}{\beta\hat{b}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{5},\ \ \frac{8\Lambda\theta\sigma^{2}b}{\mu\beta\hat{b}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{5},\quad\frac{4\Lambda\sigma^{2}b}{\mu^{2}\beta\hat{b}K}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{5},\ \ \textrm{and}\ \ \frac{6c_{0}r^{2}\sigma^{2}}{(K+r-1)^{2}\mu\theta n_{0}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{5}.\end{array}

These five conditions are simultaneously satisfied if we choose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}K:=\big\lfloor\frac{\sqrt{10}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}-1\big\rfloor,\quad\theta:=\epsilon,\quad n_{0}:=\frac{3c_{0}\mu r^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\Psi_{0}^{2}\epsilon},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\frac{\hat{b}}{b}\geq 2\sigma^{2}\max\left\{\frac{10\sqrt{10}\Lambda\Psi_{0}}{\mu\beta\epsilon},\frac{20\Lambda\sigma^{2}}{\mu\beta\epsilon},\frac{\sqrt{10}\Lambda\sigma^{2}}{\mu\beta\Psi_{0}\epsilon}\right\}.\end{array}

Combining this condition and
b_{k}=b\geq\frac{3\beta}{\mu\Lambda\theta}=\frac{3\beta}{\mu\Lambda\epsilon}
above, we conclude that
b_{k}=b:=\big\lfloor\frac{c_{b}}{\epsilon}\big\rfloor
for
c_{b}:=\frac{3\beta}{\mu\Lambda}
and

\hat{b}_{k}=\hat{b}:=\lfloor\frac{\hat{c}_{b}}{\epsilon^{2}}\rfloor
for
\hat{c}_{b}:=\frac{6\sqrt{10}\sigma^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\max\left\{10\Psi_{0},2\sqrt{10},\frac{1}{\Psi_{0}}\right\}
and all
k\geq 1
, and
n_{0}:=\big\lfloor\frac{c_{n}}{\epsilon}\big\rfloor
for
c_{n}:=\frac{3c_{0}\mu r^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\Psi_{0}^{2}}
.
Since
\epsilon\in(0,1/2]
and
\theta=\epsilon
, we also have
\theta\in(0,1/2]
.

Finally, since
b_{k}=b>0
and
\hat{b}_{k}=\hat{b}>0
for all
k\geq 0
, the expected total number of oracle calls is
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{0}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{k}\big]
, where
\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
is

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{K}\big]=&(2b+\hat{b})(K+1)=\big(\frac{2c_{b}}{\epsilon}+\frac{\hat{c}_{b}}{\epsilon^{2}}\big)\frac{\sqrt{10}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon}\leq\frac{(2c_{b}+\hat{c}_{b})\sqrt{10}\Psi_{0}}{\mu\epsilon^{3}},\end{array}\vskip-2.15277pt

and
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{0}\big]=2b+n_{0}=\mathcal{O}\big(\epsilon^{-1}\big)
is the expected total number of oracle calls for evaluating
\widetilde{F}^{0}
.
Overall, the expected total number of oracle calls
\mathbf{F}(\cdot,\xi)
and
J_{\lambda T}
evaluations of (
VFOSA+
) is at most
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}_{K}\big]:=\mathcal{O}\big(\epsilon^{-1}+\epsilon^{-3}\big)
to achieve
\mathbb{E}\big[\|G_{\lambda}x^{K}\|^{2}\big]\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
∎

Appendix E
The Convergence Analysis of Algorithm
2

This appendix provides the full proofs of the results in Section
5
.

E.1
The proof of Theorem
25
— Key estimates

Proof.

To analyze the convergence of (
VFOSA-
), we construct the following functions:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}&:=&\beta a_{k}\|S_{\lambda}u^{k}\|^{2}+t_{k-1}\langle S_{\lambda}u^{k},u^{k}-s^{k}\rangle+\frac{c_{k}}{2\nu\beta}\|s^{k}-u^{\star}\|^{2},\\
\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{k}&:=&\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}+\big[\bar{\beta}-(1+s)\beta\big]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\|S_{\lambda}u^{k}-S_{\lambda}u^{k-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{\penalty\ }\Lambda Lt_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)\langle Fx^{k}-Fx^{k-1},x^{k}-x^{k-1}\rangle,\\
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{k}&:=&\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{k}+\frac{[\mu(1-\kappa_{k})\Gamma_{k}+\beta]t_{k-1}(t_{k-1}-1)}{2\mu}\Delta_{k-1},\end{array}\vskip-2.15277pt

where
u^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(S_{\lambda})
,
a_{k}:=t_{k-1}[t_{k-1}-1-s(1-\nu)]
,
\mu\in(0,1]
,
s>0
,
c_{k}
is given in (
27
), and
\Gamma_{k}>0
are given parameters.
The quantity
\Delta_{k}
and the parameter
\kappa_{k}
are given in Definition
4
.
The functions
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{k}
, and
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{k}
are similar to the ones
\mathcal{L}_{k}
,
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
, and
\mathcal{P}_{k}
in (
26
), respectively.

In this case,
S_{\lambda}u
and
(u^{k},s^{k})
play the same role as
G_{\lambda}x
and
(x^{k},z^{k})
in Section
4
and the results of Theorem
12
still hold for
S_{\lambda}u
and
\{(u^{k},s^{k})\}
.
Using the relation
\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|=\|F(J_{\lambda T}u^{k})+\lambda^{-1}(u^{k}-J_{\lambda T}u^{k})\|=\|S_{\lambda}u^{k}\|
, we obtain (
47
) from (
36
).
The estimates in (
48
) follow from (
38
) and the relation
\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|=\|S_{\lambda}u^{k}\|
.
Finally, since
u\in J_{\lambda T}u+\lambda T(J_{\lambda T}u)
, it is obvious to check that
\xi^{k}:=\frac{1}{\lambda}(u^{k}-J_{\lambda T}u^{k})=\frac{1}{\lambda}(u^{k}-x^{k})\in T(J_{\lambda T}u^{k})=Tx^{k}
.
∎

E.2
The proof of Theorem
26
— The
\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^{2}\big)
and
o\big(1/k^{2}\big)
-rates

Proof.

The conclusions of Theorem
26
follow from the results of Theorem
13
, respectively and the relations
\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|=\|J_{\lambda T}u^{k+1}-J_{\lambda T}u^{k}\|\leq\|u^{k+1}-u^{k}\|
and
\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|=\|S_{\lambda}u^{k}\|
for
\xi^{k}:=\frac{1}{\lambda}(u^{k}-x^{k})\in Tx^{k}
.
We omit the details to avoid a repetition.
∎

E.3
The proof of Theorem
27
— Almost sure convergence

Proof.

First, the
o\big(1/k^{2}\big)
almost sure convergence rates also follow from those in Theorem
14
, respectively and the relations
\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|=\|J_{\lambda T}u^{k+1}-J_{\lambda T}u^{k}\|\leq\|u^{k+1}-u^{k}\|
and
\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|=\|S_{\lambda}u^{k}\|
for
\xi^{k}:=\frac{1}{\lambda}(u^{k}-x^{k})\in Tx^{k}
.

Next, we have
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|u^{k}-u^{\star}\|
exists almost surely for all
u^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(S_{\lambda})
as in Theorem
14
.
Moreover, we also have
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|S_{\lambda}u^{k}\|=0
almost surely as in Theorem
14
.
Applying Lemma
31
again, we conclude that
\{u^{k}\}
almost surely converges to a
\mathrm{zer}(S_{\lambda})
-valued random variable
\bar{u}^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(S_{\lambda})
.

Finally, since
x^{k}=J_{\lambda T}u^{k}
,
x^{\star}=J_{\lambda T}u^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
almost surely, and
J_{\lambda T}
is continuous (since it is nonexpansive), it is easy to show that
\{x^{k}\}
also converges to
x^{\star}
almost surely by the classical Continuous Mapping Theorem
(
Durrett, 2019
, Exercise 1.3.3)
.
∎
